  - **Production sizing ran before the decision** (`docs/claude-plans/item1-admin-authored-applicable-programs-sizing.sql`, 2026-08-11) and is recorded rather than merely cited: **A — 0 rows, 0 notes, 0 owners** in the admin-authored shape, so the class is entirely unrealised and **no cleanup migration is needed**; **B — none**; **C — none**, meaning *no non-admin-owned note carries a join row at all*, so the restriction **costs nothing measurable**; **D — 885 admin-owned / 4 other-public / 4480 other-private / 5369 total**. Zero affected users is the argument *for* prevention, exactly as it was for B2.
  - **Cheap because the Official Library is `ADMIN`-owned** (`isOfficialAuthor` is `role == ADMIN`), so the main curation workflow is an admin acting on its own notes, and teachers already curate only their own. What is given up is an admin classifying a *learner's* public note — production holds 4 such notes and none carry rows. If community authoring later makes that a real need, it returns as a scoped decision with the `source` column on the table, not as a quiet relaxation.
  - **Implementation consequence to carry into the fix PR — a UI change, not only a list filter.** The admin curator page writes through the same restricted `PUT` (`replaceNoteApplicablePrograms`), and `findAuthorizedNote` has exactly one caller, so no second path survives; the separate `AdminNoteApplicableProgramsController` is GET-only and needs no change. `getAdminPage` uses `findAll()` with no filter, so the page currently offers an edit action on all 5369 notes, 4480 of them other users' private notes — **83% of everything it renders** — and every one would begin returning `NoteNotFoundException` on a row the page just showed. Filter to the 885 writable notes rather than leaving a mostly-dead affordance.
  - **It also retires a `v0.70.0`-era known limitation** rather than leaving it standing: `V108` accepted that its *learner-owned AND not-a-copy* predicate would delete rows an admin had legitimately authored. Those rows can no longer be authored, so that cost is moot going forward. The original wording still appears under `v0.71.0` below — read it as historical.
  - **This narrows, and does not close, item 2's provenance gap.** With cross-user admin authoring prevented, a non-curator owner's rows can only arrive by copy inheritance, which `copiedFromNoteId`/`sourceNoteId` distinguish. Residue: `replace` is a full replace, so a curator acting on a note they own that is itself a copy can overwrite inherited rows while `copiedFromNoteId` stays set. Recorded, not inferred away — it is precisely the argument the `source` column makes for itself.
- **A learner sees inherited programs on their own note that they cannot edit (frontend + backend). DECIDED 2026-08-10 — ruling recorded in `ADR-001`.** The finding as filed described the visible half. The harm is the other half: **on any copy of a curator-authored note the learner's `Course / Program(s)` field is required and permanently inert.** `copyNote` copies the string, the Domain Context *and* the inherited join rows; the canonical-shapes table defines a curator-authored note's string as **null**, so the copy starts null and the required-field check fires on the learner's **first** edit — guaranteed, not an edge case. Nothing reads what they type: discovery is join-first, and generation reads the string only through `effectiveAuthoringDomain`, which is shadowed at 2+ programs by the inherited Domain Context (required on curated multi-program notes) and at exactly one program by the joined catalog name. **Two independent mechanisms, one per branch.** This is an *interaction* defect — copy inheritance and ownership-blind read semantics are each correct, and their conjunction was never examined.
  - **Ruling:** `ADR-001`'s *"No learner-facing Applicable Programs UI"* governs authoring **controls**, not provenance **display**. A learner may be shown the programs their note carries, read-only, with their provenance; they may never author, add, remove, or edit them. Withholding this was the worse option — it leaves the learner unable to explain why their own note is filed where it is.
  - **The fix predicate is `shadowed = (joinRowCount >= 1) && (joinRowCount == 1 || domainContext != null)`, not "has join rows."** The string is shadowed only when **both** readers ignore it. **This shipped wrong and was corrected at the pressure test:** the original `(joinRowCount == 1) || (domainContext != null)` dropped the discovery term and reported shadowed at *zero* join rows, where the string is the only value discovery has — freezing that learner's own field while it still drove their shelf. See the pressure-test section below.
  - **F4 folds into this branch** rather than being scoped separately — `NoteService.resolveRequestedCourseProgram` already falls back to the owner's profile program and throws only when both are null, which is the same defect on the AI-suggestion surface, closing with the same predicate. **The four deferred findings are therefore three branches, not four.**
  - **This ruling binds M2, which is in this same release. CORRECTION 2026-08-11 — the sequencing rationale recorded here was wrong, and it was mine.** It read: *"a learner sees inherited programs only on Note Detail today because the private list projection returns an empty array; the private library card already passes `applicablePrograms` through, so correcting M2 surfaces them on learner cards automatically."* Tracing it while scoping M2 showed the mechanism does not exist. **Library cards read `listLibraryPage`**, whose native query has carried the aggregate join since slice 2 (`de441fbc`) — so cards have shown joined programs for the whole of Release B. **M2 concerns `listMine` (`GET /notes`)**, a JPQL projection that no card consumes; correcting it surfaces nothing on any card. The ruling still binds M2 in the sense that both must agree on what cards show, but **the ordering was never load-bearing** and nothing depended on it. What is true and was obscured: cards already show the neutral summary with no provenance, which is what this ruling requires of them — a live state, not a future consequence.
  - **Provenance display lives on Note Detail, not on cards.** Note Detail already carries the note-level *"Copied from … in Public Library."* line, so the read-only program block leans on an existing true statement; a card carries no copy indicator, so provenance there would have to assert *how* the rows arrived — unsafe while item 1 (admin curation of a learner's note) is undecided. Cards keep the neutral program summary. **Known gap:** on an admin-curated non-copy learner note the programs appear with no explanation; the Note Detail sentence is conditioned on `copiedFromNoteId` so it stays true under either resolution of item 1.
  - **L12 folds into this branch.** The card's own comment justifies its count with *"The full list lives on Note Detail"* — it does not: Note Detail shows a count too, and the names render only inside the curator-gated combobox, so for a learner the full list has never existed anywhere. The read-only names block is L12's fix, and the `Applies to` / `Applicable to` divergence was **only partly** settled here — the branch changed was unreachable, and the live curator instance in `course-programs-viewer.tsx` was missed until the pressure test. Genuinely unified now, with no `Applicable to` left in production code. **Remove L12 from the Group 2 list rather than fixing it twice.**
  - **Copy settled 2026-08-10, full contract in `docs/features/notes.md`.** Note Detail shows middot-separated **names** under the existing `COURSE / PROGRAM(S)` label, followed by *"Set by the note this was copied from. Your own course or program is on your profile."* — the sentence conditioned on `copiedFromNoteId` so it stays true on an admin-curated non-copy note. Cards keep `Applies to N programs`, which becomes the single phrasing once Note Detail's count is replaced by names.
  - **The underlying class is deliberately not closed here** — the two representations still overlap. Both options that would close it (stop inheriting join rows on copy; let a learner's value clear them) amend ratified `ADR-001` text, which this release's anti-drift locks out. Backlog Index row added, with its sharpest evidence: a learner can flip their copy to `PUBLIC` and public discovery is join-first, so inherited curator rows can drive public shelves for a note the curator never published.
- **`NoteBulkGenerationService` still uses the bare curator predicate (backend). SHIPPED 2026-08-11.** The two note-authoring paths corrected in `adfa797f` guard on `onboardingCompletedAt` first; this third one did not. Not UI-reachable — `requireAuthenticatedOnboardedUser` redirects mid-onboarding users away from bulk generate — so this was consistency work on a rule `CLAUDE.md` states as a rule, not a live activation bug.

**Group 2 — carried Medium/Low findings, triaged.** All twenty were re-verified against current code on 2026-08-07; full `file:line` detail is in `docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-pre-signoff-pressure-test.md`. Taken here:

- **Contracts that lie about themselves.** **M2 — SHIPPED 2026-08-11** (`GET /notes` always returned `applicablePrograms: []` — the projection did not select the field its DTO advertises; no consumer read it, which is exactly why it would have been trusted wrongly later), M1 (no `@JsonAlias` on the `courseProgram` → `courseProgramIds`/`courseProgramText` rename, so a learner on a stale bundle reads their program as null), M10 (the pre-checkout draft save swallows its exception, hiding the Domain Context instruction behind a generic message).
- **Consumers still blind to the join.** M3 — `PostSessionNextStepService` skips the next-step recommendation for a curated note, `PublicProfileService` renders no program on profile cards, `DashboardService` degrades to null. M4 — Interview Practice's source picker offers the whole ready library instead of program-matched notes when the legacy string is absent.
- **Copy and control defects.** M11 (the facet explainer sits on a dropdown that renders no counts, while the Subjects facet that *does* render counts can never exceed the total), L1 (`CourseProgramCombobox` discards a caller's `placeholder`), L3 (`public-profile-page-client.tsx` is the only `SharedNoteCard` call site omitting `applicablePrograms`). **L12 moved into group 1 item 2** — the read-only names block is its fix, so fixing it separately would mean doing it twice.
- **Query-path hygiene, if the group-1 work leaves room.** L4 (no index on `notes(course_program)`, and the public slug fallback wraps it in `regexp_replace`), L5 (`array_agg(... order by name)` is the only un-gated dialect call). **Not taken:** L6 is not UI-reachable and L7 matches 0 notes per the project's own production read, so neither earns a change; **L8 — the two dead `V106` FK columns — is not a cleanup this release may do**, because the only obvious fix is a `DROP COLUMN`, which is exactly what `v0.71.0` declined on irreversibility grounds and what this section's own "migrations stay additive" rule forbids. It is recorded, not scheduled.

Anti-drift — locked, and **not** re-litigated by anything in this release:

- **No new axes, no ADR scope change.** The five `ADR-001` axes stand. Applicable Programs remains discovery-only and never reaches a prompt; Domain Context remains the sole LLM domain constraint.
- **The join-first read with legacy-string fallback stays.** `v0.71.0` recorded retiring `notes.course_program` as *"a separate, unscheduled decision."* It stays unscheduled. Nothing here makes the join the only read.
- **The four `2026-08-05` Program Family rulings stand** — the catalog represents valid applicability not curriculum coverage, families stay dumb, expansion fills all members, expansion is never subject-conditioned. No catalog seed migration ships here either.
- **C8 and C9 stay out of scope and stay carried.** Both are onboarding-vocabulary findings whose fixes belong to the Onboarding Intent Router work (`docs/claude-plans/onboarding-activation-and-intent-router.md` §3 and §1.4), not to the program axis. So do **M13**, **M15** and **M16**, which are the same vocabulary and guard question in different clothes. "Known Limitations cleanup" must not read as having absorbed them — if this release signs off without them, they are re-recorded, not closed.
- **M12 and M9 stay decisions, and neither is forced here.** M12 — whether `courseProgram` is guaranteed non-null after onboarding, and therefore whether `effectiveAuthoringDomain` should have a final fallback the way `effectiveCurriculumLevel` defaults to `COLLEGE` — is an ADR-level question, and answering it inside a patch release is how an architecture decision gets made by accident. M9 — a stale cached `profileType`/`role` in localStorage making the client send curator-shaped payloads — needs a reconciliation strategy, not a patch. Both may be *answered* here; neither may be patched around.
- **Migrations stay additive.** If group 1 concludes a provenance column is needed, it is a new `V1NN`, never an edit to `V107` or `V108`.

### Pre-signoff full pressure test — run 2026-08-11, ALL FINDINGS DISPOSITIONED

Four independent agents with **no inherited context**, briefed on scope but deliberately not on any conclusion, so they derived findings rather than confirming them: backend correctness, frontend surfaces, cross-PR interaction, and a documentation fact-check. Then synthesised through `advisor`, per `CLAUDE.md`. Fresh context was the right call for a specific reason — the same session had written the code, the docs *and* the reasoning, so an agent inheriting that context would have inherited its blind spots.

**Every blocking finding was authored in this session. That is the headline, and it is a process finding, not just a bug list.**

- **The shadowing predicate was wrong, and the error propagated into four documents, the implementation, a Codex prompt and a test that asserted it as correct.** It was derived from the two resolvers by treating their conditions as *alternatives* when the string is only shadowed if **both** readers ignore it. Result: `true` at zero join rows with a Domain Context — exactly where the string is the only value discovery has. A learner reaching that shape had their own Course / Program hidden, un-required, skipped by `update`, and permanently uneditable while still deciding their shelf. **Two agents were needed to establish it**: the frontend agent found the broken render but could not prove reachability; the backend agent independently found the write path (copying a curated note whose program the catalog excludes). Neither alone was conclusive.
- **The M3 fix was ineffective for the case it was written for.** `PostSessionNextStepService` was taught to read the join, but `ProgressReportService.filterGoalStudyPacks` — reached through the same `buildGoalNudge` call — still filtered on the legacy string alone, so every curated pack was excluded downstream. The nudge then rendered anyway (it returns unconditionally) reporting **0% mastery and 0 due concepts**, which is worse than not firing. Now join-first and batched.
- **M10's fix removed the message it was meant to preserve.** `parseApiResponse` always throws with a non-empty message, so the `else if` guarding the draft-preserved notice was dead code. Both toasts now show: the server's says what to fix, the fallback says the draft is safe.
- **A curator-only dependency could block a learner.** Note Detail fetched the course-program catalog in the same `Promise.all` as the shadow flag, unguarded, so a catalog failure left the flag `null` — hiding the learner's field and skipping its validation. Guarded the way the Note Editor already did.
- **M4 traded "too broad" for "silently empty."** Matching now compares catalog names against learner free text, which legitimately misses; with the section gated on `availableNotes.length > 0` an empty result made the whole picker vanish. It now falls back to the full ready library.
- **L12 was not actually fixed.** The `Applicable to` changed was the *unreachable* branch; `course-programs-viewer.tsx` rendered it live for every curator, pinned by a test. Now genuinely unified on `Applies to`, with no `Applicable to` left in production code.
- **A fourth curator predicate was created in one PR while another PR recorded in `CLAUDE.md` that only three existed and all carried the onboarding guard.** Neither diff showed both halves. The guard is now on `findAuthorizedNote`, which makes the claim true rather than weakening it.
- **Test integrity was the most uncomfortable finding.** Several assertions cited as proof were vacuous: `queryByLabelText("Add a course or program")` is the *curator* control's aria-label, so asserting its absence for a `STUDENT` could never fail; `queryByLabelText(/Course \/ Program/)` returned nothing either way once the label lost its `htmlFor`. Two of the three M3 fixes had unstubbed mocks returning empty, so their join-first paths were never exercised. All replaced with assertions that can fail, plus the ordinary non-shadowed learner path that had lost coverage entirely.
- **L9 had no disposition anywhere**, while the Backlog row covering all twenty carried findings was about to close declaring them handled. It now has a stated not-taken reason and its own row.
- **`v0.71.2`'s planned scope contradicted `ADR-001:74` and cited the ADR as if it supported it.** The seed bullet is removed; the ADR's own sentence names the correct trigger.

**The nameable pattern, for the next release:** predicates and causal claims derived from a partial trace, stated confidently, then propagated into several documents before anything checked them. Per-PR `/audit-diff` cannot catch it — each diff was individually coherent. What caught it was fresh context with no stake in the earlier reasoning.

### Known limitations

- **A curator on a pre-deploy bundle sees an error on the Applicable Programs control until they reload.** `GET /notes/{noteId}/applicable-programs` changed from a bare array to an object, and the client parser rejects the old shape. A response-side alias is not possible and the old bundle is already shipped, so this is deploy-window-bounded, curator-only (one curator account today), and cleared by a hard reload. Named here rather than fixed because the release's own `@JsonAlias` work (M1) took the opposite position on the same threat model for the sibling *request* endpoint — the inconsistency is deliberate and recorded, not overlooked.
- **The shadow flag is fed by two different row counts.** `NoteApplicableProgramsService.get` uses `findByNoteId().size()`, which inner-joins `course_programs`; `NoteService.update` uses `findIdsByNoteId().size()`, which reads the raw table. They can only disagree if a `course_programs` row disappears while its join rows survive, which the FK currently prevents. **Flagged for `v0.71.2`**, whose catalog-management work is the first thing that could introduce deletion.

### Signoff note — this release tripped the full-pressure-test gate

The gate fired as predicted, and **the test has now been run — findings and dispositions are in the section above.** `CLAUDE.md`'s rule triggers when *"more than one PR touched the same shared method/component"*, and **group 1 items 1 and 2 both landed in `NoteApplicableProgramsService`** — item 2 changed `get`'s response shape, item 1 then changed `findAuthorizedNote` and `getAdminPage` around it. The interaction flagged here in advance was real: item 2 made the shadowing flag authoritative for the learner UI, item 1 changed who may create the rows that flag reads, and a reviewer looking at either diff alone saw neither half. The cross-PR agent found a genuine defect at exactly that seam — a fourth curator predicate created in one PR while another PR recorded in `CLAUDE.md` that only three existed. **Recording the gate in advance is worth keeping as a habit: it made the test's brief specific instead of general.**

### Shipped

- **Shadow-aware learner Course / Program provenance (Group 1 item 2, including F4 and L12)** — `GET /notes/{noteId}/applicable-programs` now returns the Applicable Program names with one backend-resolved `courseProgramShadowed` flag using the exact shadowing predicate — `(joinRowCount >= 1) && (joinRowCount == 1 || domainContext != null)`, corrected at the pressure test from a first version that dropped the discovery term. Learners can save and apply AI metadata suggestions without supplying an unreadable personal program, while non-shadowed notes retain the required-field guard. **A shadowed note's existing string is left untouched — neither cleared nor replaced by the editor's profile program** (found in the pre-commit audit: the delivered version wrote null, which would have silently destroyed the string a pre-slice-4 curated note carries into its copy, unrecoverably if the join rows were ever removed; the clients send the note's current stored value, so client and server agree either way). Additionally: Note Detail and the Note Editor show shadowed program names as middot-separated read-only provenance, condition the settled sentence on `copiedFromNoteId`, and preserve curator controls unchanged. This also closes F4's suggestion dead-end and L12's missing full-list promise without changing cards, the private list projection (M2), generation prompts, or schema.
- **Owner-scoped Applicable Program curation (Group 1 item 1).** Replacing a note's Applicable Programs now requires the requester to **own the note and be an `ADMIN` or `TEACHER`** — `ADMIN` alone no longer grants authority over another user's note, and ownership alone never grants a learner authoring authority. Unauthorized writes stay concealed behind `NoteNotFoundException` rather than a 403 that would reveal the note exists. **The `ADMIN`-or-owner read path is deliberately left unchanged and asymmetric**, because the learner-facing read-only provenance block from item 2 depends on it; `AGENTS.md` now records that asymmetry as intentional so a later change does not "clean it up". The Admin Dashboard pages only the requesting admin's own notes via a new paged owner-scoped finder. **`ownerEmail` is removed from the admin response entirely — a response-shape change, not only a hidden column** (added in the pre-commit audit, beyond the prompt): once every row is the viewer's own note the field is a constant, and leaving an API field nothing reads would be the same *contract lies* defect this release is fixing as M2. The admin page is its sole consumer, verified before removal.
- **Group 2 sweep — M1, M3, M4, M10, M11, L1, L3 fixed; L4 and L5 measured and dispositioned.** Shipped as one branch, in a single commit (`29edf191`) — an earlier draft of this line claimed a commit per finding, which git does not show.
  - **M1 — `@JsonAlias("courseProgram")` on `UpsertNoteRequest.courseProgramText`.** The `v0.71.0` wire rename had only a *Java* constructor overload, which does nothing for JSON. A **learner** on a stale bundle had the field read as null, and `resolveRequestedCourseProgram` fell back to the **profile** program — never the note's existing value — silently reassigning the note for the length of a deploy window. Three deserialization tests.
  - **M3 + L3 — three consumers stopped being blind to the join,** through one shared `NoteEffectivePrograms` helper rather than three implementations. `PostSessionNextStepService` skipped the goal nudge entirely for curated notes (whose legacy string is null by definition) and now resolves join-first, treating a multi-program note as in-goal if **any** of its programs is — the note genuinely serves that goal. `DashboardService`'s Continue Studying resolves join-first, returning null for a multi-program note rather than picking one arbitrarily. `PublicProfileService` gained a batched program lookup and `PublicProfileNoteResponse.applicablePrograms`, which is also **L3's fix** — that finding was filed as frontend-only, but the DTO could not carry programs, so it needed the backend too.
  - **M4 — Interview Practice offers program-matched sources again, and never an empty picker.** It read `courseProgram` alone, so a curated note left the filter matching everything. It now resolves both sides join-first and matches on any shared program, reading the current note's programs **out of the same `listNotes` payload** — which carries them since M2 — rather than issuing a second request. No resolvable program still offers everything, rather than an empty picker.
  - **M10 — the pre-checkout save stops swallowing its exception, and still says the draft is safe.** A curator hitting the paywall with several programs and blank Domain Context now sees the server's actual instruction; the generic draft-preservation notice remains for infrastructure failures.
  - **M11 — the facet explainer is true where it sits.** It claimed "these counts can exceed the note total" under a dropdown that renders **no counts**, while the Subjects facet that *does* render counts is single-valued and can never exceed. Moving it to Subjects would have made it false, so the copy now describes what that filter actually does: *"A note can apply to several programs, so it appears under each one it serves."* **Two tests were pinning the false claim** and were corrected with it.
  - **L1 — `CourseProgramCombobox` honours a caller's `placeholder` in both modes.** The default lived on the prop, so `placeholder` was never undefined and the `allowCustom={false}` branch overwrote whatever the caller passed — discarding states like "Loading course programs…" that a caller supplies precisely because it knows something the component does not.
  - **L4 — the EXPLAIN the finding asked for has now been run, and it changes the recommendation.** The public slug fallback is a **Seq Scan**, and the reason a plain `notes(course_program)` index would not help is that the predicate wraps the column in `regexp_replace` — it needs an **expression** index, which is a larger decision than the finding assumed. Measured locally only (121 notes vs production's 5,369), so no migration ships here; recorded with its evidence instead of guessed at.
  - **L9 — not taken, with a reason (added at the pressure test, which found it had no disposition at all).** `notes.status` is `not null` in three test schemas and nullable in two, across a shared JVM-lifetime H2 using `create table if not exists` — so the first schema to load wins and the rest silently inherit it. **Not taken because the fix is a test-infrastructure decision, not a patch:** the schemas need consolidating behind one definition, which is a change to how every integration test boots and is exactly the kind of thing that turns a green suite red for unrelated reasons mid-release. Currently green; the risk is order-dependence, so the failure mode is a confusing future breakage rather than anything user-facing. **Carried with its own Backlog Index row** rather than closed. This was the only one of the twenty carried findings the `v0.71.1` triage never mentioned in either direction — the sweep accounted for 19 of 20, and the Backlog row was about to close declaring all of them handled.
  - **L5 — left as recorded.** `array_agg(... order by name)` diverges between PG collation and H2 code-point ordering only on the four U+2013 catalog names, which no test uses. Changing it would fix nothing observable.
- **`GET /notes` stops advertising a field it never filled (M2).** `listMine` ran on a JPQL projection that never selected `applicablePrograms` — JPQL cannot express the `array_agg` the join needs — while the Library page's native query always did, so one endpoint returned real programs and the other returned `[]` for the same DTO. `listMine` now uses the same `NOTE_LIST_ITEM_SELECT + NOTE_LIST_ITEMS_FROM` constants, so the two cannot drift; **no new select or join was written** (a `where owner / order by updated_at desc` fragment was added). The dead JPQL projection is deleted rather than left as a second, silently-lossy way to list notes. The aggregate is owner-scoped, so it is empty for every learner (no non-admin note carries a join row in production) and bounded for a curator. **Regression coverage is an integration test, not a unit test** — the mapper was never the broken part, so only a real query can catch this; two were added to `NoteServiceListProjectionIntegrationTest`, one asserting joined names come back and one asserting the left join does not drop programme-less notes.
  - **A `v0.71.1` record correction came out of scoping this.** The item 2 entry claimed cards would start showing inherited programs when M2 landed, and made that sequencing load-bearing. Tracing it showed the mechanism does not exist: library cards read `listLibraryPage`, whose native query has carried the join since slice 2, so cards have shown joined programs for the whole of Release B; M2 concerns `listMine`, which no card consumes. Corrected in `ADR-001`, `RELEASES.md` and `ROADMAP.md` — the ordering was a correct instinct on a wrong mechanism, and nothing depended on it.
- **The curator onboarding guard now covers all three authoring paths (Group 1 item 3).** `NoteBulkGenerationService` was the last holdout on the bare role check that `adfa797f` corrected elsewhere; it now returns `false` for `onboardingCompletedAt == null` before applying the role check, matching `NoteService.isTeacherSelectableOwner` and `NoteGenerationService.isCurator`. Not UI-reachable, so this changes no user-visible behaviour — the value is that `CLAUDE.md` states this as a rule and a rule with a live in-repo exception decays. **The existing tests proved the guard has teeth rather than being decorative:** the suite's `mockUser` helper never set `onboardingCompletedAt`, so every curator test was running against a mid-onboarding user and would have silently flipped to the learner branch. The helper now models an onboarded user, which is the only state the surface can actually be in, plus a dedicated mid-onboarding helper for the new case. Two tests added — one asserting a curator-role account mid-onboarding takes the learner branch, one scope guard asserting an onboarded curator is not demoted. **`CLAUDE.md` corrected:** it still named `NoteBulkGenerationService` as carrying the bare form. This closes the ordinary product mechanism that could recreate the state `V108` removed — with **no migration, schema change, or provenance column**, because production sizing found zero affected rows before the decision was made.

## v0.71.0 - Applicable Programs

**Status: Released** (signed off 2026-08-10)

Theme: a canonical note stops belonging to one program. `note_course_program` makes applicability a many-to-many fact, so an Algebra note authored once for Engineering Mathematics can surface under every engineering program that needs it — which is the payoff Release A built the foundations for.

This is **Release B of `docs/architecture/ADR-001-canonical-knowledge-architecture.md`**. Release A closed with `v0.70.0`.

**Read ADR-001's own warnings before scoping anything here.** Release B is materially riskier than Release A, and the ADR says so in its own Consequences section:

- **It is not reversible** once filters and badges read the join; rollback would require a migration. This knowingly fails clause 2 of the ROADMAP bootstrap test and is accepted as a multi-release commitment.
- **Filter and search paths move to join/`EXISTS` semantics on a hot paginated path** that already required a dedicated performance release (`v0.51.0`).
- **Facet counts will sum above the note total**, because a note appears under every applicable program. That is correct behavior needing a UI affordance, not a bug to fix.
- **Program Families are an authoring shortcut only** — selecting a family expands to explicit `note_course_program` rows at save time. Applicability is never inferred from a family at read time.

### Planned Scope

Cut into **three slices along the irreversibility boundary**, not by layer — full reasoning in `docs/claude-prompt/canonical-knowledge-architecture-out/18-release-b-slice-sequence.md`. Release A split by layer because all of it was reversible; Release B is not.

**Slice 3's former gate is cleared.** The opening framing of this section read "blocked on the applicability question," which is a broader reading than ADR-001's own gate: the ADR required curator verification *"before **family-expansion defaults** are set"* — not before the join table, the backfill, or a curator adding programs to a note by hand. The 2026-08-05 owner rulings narrowed family expansion to an unconditional authoring shortcut, removing the curriculum question from the implementation.

- **Slice 1 — `note_course_program`, 1:1 backfill, admin write surface (backend + migration + frontend).** Additive, reversible, gated on nothing. Backfills exactly one row per note from the existing string resolved against the `v0.70.0` catalog; a note whose value the catalog deliberately excluded gets **no** join row and keeps its string. Nothing reads the join. **This is the slice that delivers what the whole ADR exists for** — one canonical note applicable to many programs, no duplication.
- **Slice 2 — filters, facets, badges, and search move to join/`EXISTS` (backend).** The irreversible commitment, and a hot paginated path that already needed a dedicated performance release (`v0.51.0`). **Must run while every note still has exactly one program row**, because that is what makes it testable: facet counts are `count(*)` grouped on `notes` today, so the join version returns identical counts and the regression test is "same filter, same counts, before and after" rather than a judgment call. Once notes carry several programs, facet counts sum above the note total — correct per ADR-001, needing a UI affordance rather than a fix.
- **Slice 3 — Program Family expansion (frontend).** Gate cleared. The shared Applicable Programs control adds every catalog member of a family to the explicit selection, which the author can trim before the existing save path persists it; no backend or catalog change is involved.
- **Slice 4 — Single Program Axis: remove "Primary Course / Program" (backend + frontend).** One `Course / Program(s)` picker replaces the two-field model across Note Editor, Note Detail inline edit, Create-from-topic, and Bulk Generate. Learners use one free-text personal program stored in `notes.course_program`; Teacher/Admin curation uses one-or-many catalog ids stored in `note_course_program`. Domain Context is required above one program on both save endpoints, and program lists never reach the LLM. Copies inherit both representations; no migration or catalog seed is needed.
- **Slice 5 — Onboarding Intent Router (frontend + backend). Added 2026-08-06 after the pre-signoff pressure test, by owner ruling.** The pressure test found onboarding to be the flow most severely affected by this architecture change, including **B0 — an activation-blocking regression this release introduced**: `resolveRequestedCourseProgram` gained a `throw` where `main` returned null, and onboarding creates its first note without sending a program, so every new user on the create-note path receives *"Choose at least one course or program."* on a screen with no such field and cannot finish onboarding. Scoped in `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-activation-and-intent-router.md`. Five parts, in order: **(1)** the B0 repair, independent and landing first so signoff is never blocked behind the redesign; **(2)** an owner-run production audit of Official Review Set program vocabulary (`onboarding-review-set-vocabulary-audit.sql`); **(3)** activation repair — persist Profile Type at Step 1 and learning context at Step 2 through a new narrow `PUT /users/profile/learning-context`, replacing a fire-and-forget write that can silently lose a user's learner level and program; **(4)** a program-resolution layer shaped by the audit; **(5)** the Intent Router itself — a first-intent step whose supporting copy resolves against the learner's own program *before* selection, practice-first opened beyond `BOARD_EXAM`, and an honest availability state for unsupported programs. **Governing philosophy, owner-ratified:** onboarding is no longer profile collection — it is an intelligent router that helps every learner reach the fastest successful study experience available to them. **Deliberately scoped in rather than deferred:** onboarding is a one-time flow, so unlike slice 4 there is no re-learning cost to deferring — the owner's reason is that shipping a first impression that models the wrong product is worse than the added scope.

**The gate on slice 3 — CLEARED 2026-08-05 by owner ruling.** It read: *applicability groupings are unverified against current PRC board syllabi; `[EFFORT]`, not `[EVIDENCE]` — no query answers whether `Engineering Sciences` spans 8 or 11 engineering programs.* It was cleared by narrowing the question rather than answering it as posed: the catalog holds **3** engineering programs and one family, not 11 (the figure came from an early doc reasoning about Philippine engineering education generally), and the rulings make expansion **unconditional**, so no subject→program mapping is needed and the syllabus question stops gating anything. **R4 still did not settle applicability** — that caveat stands, it is simply no longer load-bearing.

Four rulings, none of which slice 3 may re-litigate. Full text in ADR-001 and `docs/product/ROADMAP.md`:

1. **The catalog represents *valid applicability*, not curriculum coverage** — "who can legitimately study this note?" Review Sets communicate completeness. **The catalog still follows curriculum, but a program no longer needs a complete Review Set to earn an entry** — it earns one once legitimate canonical notes are applicable to it. **Pre-seeding every PRC engineering program is rejected as premature**; growth is incremental and demand-driven by authoring. **So slice 3 ships no catalog seed migration** — the 21 seeded programs stand until authoring demands more. Refines rather than reverses the `v0.70.0` follow-not-lead posture; the `Computer Science` / `Software Engineering` rulings stand.
2. **Program Families stay intentionally dumb** — an authoring shortcut, never a curriculum engine.
3. **Expansion fills in all family members** — no curated subsets; the author trims.
4. **Expansion is never subject-conditioned** — explicitly rejected, because it would re-couple Subject knowledge to applicability rules.

**Binding principle, now in ADR-001:** Program Families are a **productivity feature, not a curriculum feature**, deliberately allowed to over-select because the Note's explicit Applicable Programs are always the source of truth.

**Slice 2's stated safety property was falsified after slice 1 shipped, and its semantics changed — owner ruling, 2026-08-05.** The scope above says the join returns identical facet counts at one row per note. It does not: `V107` deliberately creates no join row for a catalog-*excluded* value, so a pure-join rewrite changes **result sets**, not counts — an excluded value's facet disappears, its filter returns 0 notes instead of N, and its public shareable slug URL stops resolving. **Slice 2 therefore reads the join with a legacy-string fallback** (`EXISTS(join rows) OR (no join rows AND legacy string matches)`), which is identical to today's behavior at 1:1 and correct once a note carries several programs. Accepted cost: `notes.course_program` stays load-bearing on read paths, making slice 2 a smaller irreversible step than originally scoped — the join becomes the primary read, not the only one. Retiring the fallback is a separate, unscheduled decision. Production impact sizing: `19-slice-2-facet-equivalence-impact.sql`, query A. Full correction: `18-release-b-slice-sequence.md`.

### Record corrections made at signoff

The signoff audit derived what shipped from `git log origin/main..HEAD` rather than from these bullets, and found gaps the slice-level bullets never covered — the same pattern that lost four bullets on 2026-08-06: work landing *after* its slice's PR merged is never revisited.

- **Breaking request-shape change on `POST /notes` and `PUT /notes/{id}`** (`595dfa62`): `UpsertNoteRequest.courseProgram` became `courseProgramIds: List<UUID>` **plus** `courseProgramText: String`, and two new 400 contracts appeared — `CourseProgramSelectionRequiredException` and `MultiProgramDomainContextRequiredException`. Previously visible only obliquely inside Known Limitation M1.
- **Bulk Generate was part of slice 4** (`NoteBulkGenerationService` +121, `bulk-generation-page-client.tsx` +83) but appeared only in Planned Scope, never in Shipped.
- **Diagnostic Read Round 2** (`090caa7f`) was recorded in `ROADMAP.md` only, while the slice 5 narrative silently depends on its gate discharge. Result: **W1→W2 retention 0.91% (1 of 110), 0 of 74 open-ended learners returned**, create-first vs practice-first **inconclusive** (0/33 vs 1/18) with its window now permanently closed. Instrumentation was verified healthy first. **Nothing in it validates the Intent Router** — the gap it identifies is a *return trigger*, not an entry point, deliberately scoped outside this release.
- **Query A production result** (`43f1be4e`): 1.17% of notes (56 of 4,787) sit outside the catalog, every one a deliberate exclusion. The sizing query was cited as a mechanism but its result was never recorded here.
- **Documentation sweep — nine files corrected**, all cases of a doc actively asserting something this release made false, which is worse than an omission because the anti-drift files are what a future session trusts:
  - `AGENTS.md` — the authoring-domain chain was documented as 3 steps, missing the `note_course_program` step. **This was the same defect already fixed in `CLAUDE.md`, surviving in the anti-drift file** — the one place it does the most damage.
  - `CLAUDE.md` — the bare curator role check, which `onboarding.md` explicitly forbids restoring, was still presented as *the* rule with no mention of the onboarding guard in front of it.
  - `SPEC.md` — two retired blocks: the single-axis "`courseProgram` is the top-level track" hierarchy `ADR-001` exists to replace, and the claim that the catalog is *"derived from saved values, not from a separate `course_programs` table"*.
  - `DATA_MODEL.md` — `note_course_program` was entirely absent. Added with its schema, `V107`/`V108`'s division of labour, join-first read semantics, and the exactly-one-row rule for generation.
  - `dashboard.md` — called the onboarding flow *"locked and unchanged"* on the flow slice 5 had just rebuilt.
  - `onboarding.md` — four stale claims: three "recorded as defects, fix pending" items that slice 5 actually fixed, and the `BOARD_EXAM`-only practice-first branch. Also gained the missing analytics section.
  - `quiz.md` — the falsified "cards, not quiz content" LaTeX exclusion, plus the older "working-solution panel only" claim it contradicted.
  - `identification.md` — the symbolic-form rule had been written into `quiz.md` only, leaving the format's own feature doc silent on the constraint that governs it.
  - `onboarding/page.tsx` — a code comment claiming the deferred marker retries completion. It never did; the retry is deferred, the false claim is not.

### Known Limitations

**Four signoff-pressure-test findings are deferred to `v0.71.1`** — each needs a decision rather than a patch. Full detail: `docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-signoff-pressure-test.md`.

- **`NoteApplicableProgramsService.replace` can recreate the `V108` class post-deploy.** An admin may set catalog programs on any learner's note, and a learner update never clears them — the same permanent override, by ordinary admin action. Architectural: fixing `V108` alone does not close it.
- **A learner sees inherited programs on their own note that they cannot edit.** The applicable-programs fetch is gated on the note id rather than on curator status, so a learner on a copied curated note is shown programs, forced to fill a required Course / Program, and that value then has no visible effect — discovery still reads the inherited rows. **Touches the ratified "do not expose Applicable Programs to learners" ruling**, so it needs a product answer.
- **`NoteBulkGenerationService` still uses the bare curator predicate**, unlike the two corrected in `adfa797f`. Not reachable through the UI, since `requireAuthenticatedOnboardedUser` redirects mid-onboarding users away from bulk generate and the editor — the inconsistency survives regardless.
- **AI-suggestion apply dead-ends on a copy of a curated note** for accounts with a null profile program: it sends the stored value rather than a form field, so there is no field on that surface to fix it. Narrow population.

Also carried: **the deferred-completion marker is never retried.** Nothing re-POSTs `completeOnboarding`; the marker only suppresses the redirect, so a failed completion leaves the server's `onboardingCompletedAt` null, masked by a localStorage key scoped to one browser. Pre-existing, but this release added a new call site. **The comment claiming a retry is corrected now; the retry itself is deferred.** Verified not to affect the Diagnostic Read, which keys on the analytics event — fired in `finally` on every path — not on `onboardingCompletedAt`.

**Twenty Medium/Low pressure-test findings are carried, not dropped — candidates for `v0.71.1`.** Each was re-verified against current code on 2026-08-07 rather than copied forward from the pressure test, and none is reachable-and-harmful enough to hold a release this size. Full detail and `file:line` for every one is in `docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-pre-signoff-pressure-test.md`.

- **M1** — the `courseProgram` → `courseProgramIds`/`courseProgramText` wire rename has no `@JsonAlias`, so a **learner on a stale bundle** has their program read as null. Self-heals on reload; no data is destroyed.
- **M2** — `GET /notes` always returns `applicablePrograms: []`; the private list projection does not select the field its DTO advertises. **No consumer reads it**, so impact is nil today — but the contract lies.
- **M3** — three untouched consumers are blind to the join: `PostSessionNextStepService` skips the next-step recommendation for a curated note, `PublicProfileService` renders no program on profile cards, `DashboardService` degrades to null. None crash.
- **M4** — Interview Practice's source picker offers the user's whole ready library instead of program-matched notes when the legacy string is absent.
- **M9** — a stale cached `profileType`/`role` in localStorage can make the client send curator-shaped payloads the server rejects. Needs a reconciliation strategy, not a patch.
- **M10** — the pre-checkout draft save swallows its exception, so a curator hitting the paywall with several programs and blank Domain Context sees a generic message instead of the Domain Context instruction.
- **M11** — the facet explainer ("counts can exceed the note total") sits on the Course / Program dropdown, which renders **no counts**; the Subjects facet that does render counts can never exceed the total.
- **M12** — `effectiveAuthoringDomain` has no final fallback and can return null, while `effectiveCurriculumLevel` defaults to `COLLEGE`. **This is an open architecture question** — whether `courseProgram` is guaranteed non-null after onboarding — not a bug to patch. It needs an ADR-level answer.
- **M13** — onboarding never touches exam goals, so a reviewer who types a catalog name may not match slug-tagged public plans.
- **M15** — onboarding, `/profile` and the dashboard lightweight prompt disagree on the same field: a user who onboards with a custom program later gets a completion prompt they **cannot satisfy** without leaving for `/profile`. Related to C8; belongs with the same vocabulary work.
- **M16** — `OnboardingGuardService` checks only `profileType` and `onboardingCompletedAt`, never `learnerLevel` or `courseProgram`. **Its disposition needs rewriting rather than copying forward:** slice 5 changed the context, since the curator branch now keys on `onboardingCompletedAt` too, which makes the exemption load-bearing in a way it was not when "no change recommended" was written.
- **L1** — `CourseProgramCombobox` discards a caller's `placeholder`.
- **L3** — `public-profile-page-client.tsx` is the only `SharedNoteCard` call site omitting `applicablePrograms`.
- **L4** — no index on `notes(course_program)`, and the public slug fallback wraps it in `regexp_replace`.
- **L5** — `array_agg(... order by name)` is the only un-gated dialect call (PG collation vs H2 code-point ordering).
- **L6** — `?courseProgram=---` normalises to empty and matches every public note with no program. **Not UI-reachable.**
- **L7** — `V107`'s `Bsed → Education` alias matches 0 notes per the project's own production read.
- **L8** — two dead FK columns from `V106` that nothing writes.
- **L9** — `notes.status` nullability differs across test schemas sharing a JVM-lifetime H2.
- **L12** — copy divergence between cards and Note Detail on how program reach is phrased.

- **C8 and C9 are deliberately deferred, not dropped — both are onboarding findings whose fixes belong to the onboarding work, not to the program axis.** **C8:** onboarding offers 31 course/program suggestions (`frontend/lib/learning-profile.ts`) against a 21-row catalog (`V106`), with only 16 overlapping — so a teacher can pick **Computer Science** at onboarding, an option onboarding itself offers, then find New Note's curator pre-fill silently skips it (correctly — it requires an exact catalog match) and leaves *"No course programs selected."* on a required field their actual discipline is not in. **Constraining onboarding to the catalog is the wrong fix**: it would make onboarding unsatisfiable for a teacher whose field is off-catalog, recreating B0 somewhere new. The plan resolves it at *authoring* time instead. **Re-recorded at signoff, per the rule below — partially covered, and the remaining half is the half C8 was actually about.** What shipped is C5: the editor no longer *contradicts itself*, since the sticky bar stopped claiming *"Tailored for: Software Engineering"* while zero programs were selected. What did **not** ship is §3's authoring-time affordance: a curator whose profile program is off-catalog still gets bare *"No course programs selected."* with no explanation of why their own discipline was skipped, asserted by a passing test (`note-editor-page-client.test.tsx`, *"does not preselect a profile program the catalog does not carry"*). The user is no longer misled; they are still stuck without being told why. **C9 — re-recorded at signoff, per the rule below; its stated blocker is gone but the finding is not fixed.** Step 2's Course / Program is still byte-identical for all four profile types (*"It sets the academic domain your notes and quizzes are written for"*), so a teacher is still asked for a free-text program that can never be a note program under the two-mode model. Learner Level directly above it *is* teacher-adapted, which makes the inconsistency more visible, not less. **The sequencing blocker no longer applies:** slice 5 moved `profileType` to Step 1, so Step 2 now knows it is talking to a curator — what remains is copy work nobody scoped, and it should be re-triaged on effort rather than carried as blocked. Carried in `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-activation-and-intent-router.md` (§3 for C8's authoring-time affordance, §1.4 for C9's persistence collision) and indexed under "Onboarding Intent Router" in `ROADMAP.md`. **If slice 5 ships without covering them, they must be re-recorded here at signoff rather than closed.**

### Carried forward

- **The two `v0.70.0` Known Limitations**, both documented rather than fixed: the Challenge bank orphaning on a learner-level correction (including the write path whose duplicate-key failure surfaces at commit, outside its own catch, contradicting `docs/features/challenge-quiz.md`'s best-effort claim), and the null-by-null pool/bank cohort (40 pools, 15 bank rows). Each now has a ROADMAP Backlog Index row.
- **Regeneration is not structure-preserving** — observed during R4, logged with its own Backlog Index row.

### Pre-signoff pressure test — run 2026-08-06, ALL FINDINGS DISPOSITIONED

The full pressure test required by `CLAUDE.md` ran against the whole release (112 files, 17 non-merge commits).
**Full findings: `docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-pre-signoff-pressure-test.md`.** Every finding below is now
fixed or explicitly recorded as a Known Limitation, per `CLAUDE.md`; each carries its disposition in place.

**A SECOND pressure test ran 2026-08-09** against the final state, because the first ran against `b75c49c8` —
five slices, two migrations and a flow restructure earlier. Four independent cold-context agents. It found a
**shipping migration bug** (`V108` preserved the rows it exists to delete) plus five defects in code written
during the same week, none of which was visible in the diff that introduced it. Full findings and the four
deferrals: `docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-signoff-pressure-test.md`.

Five blockers, ranked:

- **B0 — onboarding is a dead end for every new user.** Release-introduced. **FIXED — see the Shipped section below.** The remaining slice 5 scope (early persistence, the Intent Router) is independent of the repair.
- **B1 — `copyNote` throws a foreign-key violation**, proven empirically with a probe test: `NoteService.java:352-353` (cited as `:339-340` before the C1 fix shifted the file) calls `save()` then a raw-JDBC child insert with no `flush()` between, unlike create (`:193-194`) and update (`:244-245`) which both flush. Measured: 18 of 40 public notes locally (45%) carry join rows, so "Copy to my library" 500s on them, and study-plan adoption silently yields an empty plan. ~~**Do not fix in isolation**~~ — **unblocked 2026-08-06 now that B2 shipped first.** The `flush()` makes copy succeed, and after `V108` what it propagates is curator-authored and inherited rows only, which is the intended behaviour.
- **B2 — V107's unfiltered backfill would freeze the Course / Program of every learner note it touches.** Found independently by three agents. A learner's edit would be permanently inert on both discovery and generation. **DECIDED 2026-08-06 — Option 7, prevent the mechanical derivation.** The sizing gate closed without being run: production never received `note_course_program` (`V107` ships unreleased; production stopped at `V106`), so the affected count is 0 by construction — the owner's pre-set "prevent future divergence, no UI" threshold. Ratified doctrine, now in `ADR-001` → *Representation authority*: **a learner's personal free-text Course / Program must not be mechanically materialized into a catalog Applicable Program row.** Deliberately narrower than "learner-owned notes cannot carry join rows", which would contradict copy inheritance — learner notes keep rows of legitimate curated provenance, and read semantics never consult ownership. Learner-save synchronization was rejected because it cannot distinguish a derived row from an inherited curator-authored one when both match the learner's old string. **Fixed by `V108__remove_derived_learner_note_programs.sql`**, an additive migration (never an edit to `V107`, whose checksum has already been applied elsewhere) that deletes `note_course_program` rows whose note is both learner-owned (`users.role <> 'ADMIN' AND users.profile_type IS DISTINCT FROM 'TEACHER'`) and not a copy (`copied_from_note_id IS NULL AND source_note_id IS NULL`). Condition 2 is exact rather than a `created_at` heuristic: copy inheritance is the only legitimate route to a join row on a learner note, so curator-authored and inherited rows both survive. The `DELETE` is kept outside the PL/pgSQL reporting block so the migration test actually exercises it — the trap that left `V107`'s own backfill untested (finding B4). **Known limitation:** an ADMIN can curate another user's note through the shared Applicable Programs endpoint, so rows an admin authored on a *non-copy learner-owned* note are removed by this migration. Unreachable in production (no traffic between `V107` and `V108`) and deliberately not chased with a `source` provenance column.
- **B3 — curated notes are invisible to the non-paginated public path. FIXED — see the Shipped section below.** This also covered B3b's public-note rail/callout and B3c's entire Exam Hub note list, which was found after the original pressure test.
- **B4 — the test suite cannot catch any of the above. PARTIALLY FIXED; the residue is recorded below as a Known Limitation.** Shape B now has real integration coverage (`NoteServicePublicLibraryPaginationIntegrationTest`, added with B3), `V108` has a genuine migration test with a structural guard against the `DO $$` stripping that left `V107` untested, and six tests that asserted defects as contracts were corrected. **Still open:** `NoteCourseProgramRepository` remains mocked in the other integration tests, and `@BeforeEach` still deletes join rows before notes. This was the only blocker with no disposition at all — the gap that prompted the second pressure test to check for exactly this. Shape B (join rows + NULL legacy string), the shape slice 4 made canonical, appears in **no** test; the join repository is `@MockBean` in all five integration tests; every `@BeforeEach` deletes join rows before notes, designing out cascade coverage; and V107's backfill sits inside its `DO $$` block, which the repo's own migration-test helper strips. Two blockers are additionally *locked in* by tests asserting the broken behaviour.

Also found: 9 High (including a falsified `RELEASES.md` LaTeX claim — ~10 quiz surfaces still render raw, and the shared answer-review **explanation** is raw across 5 call sites), 16 Medium, 12 Low. **Four claims in the handoff brief were disproven**, including a "removed compatibility constructor" that never existed in any commit and an H2/V107 schema divergence that does not exist. ADR-001's founding constraint was verified to genuinely hold: both resolver guards are strict `size() == 1`, so a multi-program note can never send a list to a prompt.

### Shipped

- **Pressure-test backend fixes — a stale suggestion source and a test that proved nothing (backend).** **F6:** `listMineCoursePrograms` still unioned the *unguarded* personal-string query, so the private editor's own Course / Program datalist kept offering a stale value from a note whose join rows say something else — the identical defect C2 fixed for the public vocabulary, one surface later. It now uses an owner-scoped twin of the same `NOT EXISTS` query. It had **no test at all**, which is why the C2 fix never revealed it; reverting the wiring failed nothing. **F2:** `resolve_neverTurnsMultipleJoinedProgramsIntoAnLlmDomain` left `userRepository.findById` unstubbed, so Mockito returned `Optional.empty()` and both assertions passed **because the user was not found**, not because of the multi-program rule they claimed to prove — every other test in that class stubs it. Rewritten to exercise the production path, and split in two: one asserting the binding ADR-001 guarantee (a program *list* never becomes the authoring domain — Domain Context, mandatory above one program, is what reaches the prompt), and one recording what a pre-invariant multi-program note without a Domain Context actually resolves to. That fallback is the author's *profile* program, which is why a bulk-generate result row displays that value for such a note — cosmetic, and now documented rather than silently changed.
- **Pressure-test frontend fixes — five defects in code this release shipped (frontend).** **The intent router lied about Review Set availability after any refresh.** `draft.intent` and `draft.reviewSetAvailable` persist to localStorage but `practiceFirstPlan` is component state, set only by the Step 2 submit — so reloading on step 3, which is exactly where learners pause, dropped the plan while the draft still said a set was available. One click on "Study with ready-made materials" then produced *"We're still building an Official Review Set for {Program}"*, contradicting the state the screen was painted from, and two of the three exits offered there complete onboarding and navigate away — so the learner left on a false basis. It also inflated `ONBOARDING_V2_INTENT_UNSUPPORTED_VIEWED` with every resumed session. The plan is now re-resolved on reaching step 3 without one, with a holding state so the learner never falls through to the path they did not choose, and the lookup still fails open. **The "Explore related public notes" fallback built a URL the public library cannot parse:** it emitted the raw program value where every other producer emits a slug, and the consumer resolves via `resolvePublicLibraryValueBySlug` — so `Nursing` never matched `nursing`. The API still filtered while the UI showed no active filter, no way to clear it, and the next Apply silently dropped the program. Now goes through the canonical `buildPublicLibraryUrl` with a slug. **`$10-$20` rendered as a subtraction.** The C4 currency rule rejected only whitespace before a closing `$`, so an operator satisfied it and `"10-"` was captured as LaTeX — which KaTeX renders happily, since a trailing binary operator is legal, so the error fallback never fired and both dollar signs vanished. A `$` after an operator is now a new amount. **The LaTeX sweep was falsified a third time:** flashcards, memorization and the unauthenticated public flashcards preview render `item.explanation` straight off the quiz array via `buildFlashcardDeck`, so the earlier "cards, not quiz content" exclusion — which this release wrote into `docs/features/quiz.md` — was factually wrong. All three now route through `renderMathText`. **`renderMathText` gained a null guard**, removing a crash class the sweep introduced by replacing ~15 bare `{value}` expressions that React had rendered harmlessly as nothing.
- **V108 corrected — the migration was preserving the rows it exists to delete (migration).** Found by the pre-signoff pressure test, in a migration this release shipped. `V108` keyed its DELETE on "learner-owned **and not a copy**", reasoning that copy-inheritance is the only legitimate route to a join row on a learner note. That conflated *"is a copy"* with *"has inherited rows"*, and it was wrong: `NoteService.copyNote` sets `source_note_id` on **every** copy — self-copy and public copy alike — and has done so since long before this release. So every pre-existing copy was skipped while still carrying the row `V107` derived from its own `course_program` string, leaving exactly the B2 defect `V108` was written to prevent: a derived row that overrides the program the learner's own editor shows, with no product surface able to remove it. Copy-inheritance of join rows is itself new in this release, so at migration time a pre-existing copy cannot *have* inherited rows — the copy check protected nothing that existed yet. **The predicate is now the exact inverse of `V107`'s insert**: delete a learner-owned row whose catalog name still equals what `V107` would have derived from that note's string, mirroring `V107`'s own `Bsed → Education` alias. Not a heuristic — it removes precisely `V107`'s output and nothing else, and deletes by row id so a note carrying both a derived and an inherited row loses only the derived one. Measured against real data: the new predicate removes **8 rows, all on copies**, and preserves the **3** whose catalog name differs; the old one removed **none** of the 8. **The residual check no longer re-runs the delete predicate** — that circularity is why it reported success regardless of what survived, and it now counts all rows remaining on learner-owned notes instead. The test was rewritten too: it previously asserted a row on a learner-owned copy **must survive**, encoding the defect, and now fails against the old predicate.
- **Pre-signoff Medium/Low sweep — five fixed, twenty carried (frontend + docs).** All 28 remaining pressure-test findings were re-verified against current code rather than trusted from the original write-up; three had already been fixed in passing (**M14** by the LaTeX sweep, **L11** by slice 5 stage 2, **L10** by `.gitignore`). Five more are fixed here, two of them chosen because *this release had actively made them worse*. **M7 — the review screen misreported the learner's own answer.** The choice order is a deterministic shuffle seeded on the question, but the review screen seeded on `${question}-${originalIndex}` while live sessions seed on the question alone and Long Exam used a third variant — so the letters moved between answering and reviewing, and someone who picked **B** was told *"Your Answer: D"*. Grading was never wrong, since it tracks `canonicalIndex`; that made it worse rather than better, because the product looked broken while behaving correctly. All nine call sites now seed identically on the question text, which is the only key both contexts share — the index cannot be part of it, because a session's active index and the original index are different numbers. **M8 — rendered math was invisible to screen readers.** KaTeX ran with `output: "html"`, emitting `aria-hidden` markup and no MathML. That was narrow while it affected working solutions only; the C3 sweep in this same release extended it to **every question, option and explanation in the app**. Now `htmlAndMathml`, at no visual cost. **M6** — `docs/features/study-pack-generation.md` and `CLAUDE.md` both still documented the pre-`v0.71.0` three-step resolver chain, omitting the joined-catalog step; load-bearing, because the same doc instructs services not to reconstruct the chain, so the next Codex prompt would have rebuilt the old one. **M5** — the admin Applicable Programs screen had no client-side zero-selection guard, unlike all four sibling call sites, so an admin met a raw 400 for a rule the screen could have stated. **L2** — the combobox footer claimed programs "never affect Study Pack generation", which is false: a single joined program can inform the writing domain.
- **B3/B3b/B3c fixed — non-paginated public discovery reads joined programs (backend + frontend).** The legacy-shaped `GET /notes/public` response now maps its already-ordered entity ids through the existing list-item projection, so curator notes with join rows and a NULL personal-note string carry their programs into Dashboard community results, server-rendered related-note rails, cards, and Exam Hubs. In-memory filter/search and Node-side matching use the same join-first, personal-string fallback as the paginated path; mixed-shape notes ignore stale scalar values. Public note pages select the first joined program only for their single contextual rail/callout link. B3c — the whole Exam Hub note list — was found on 2026-08-06 after the original pressure test. No endpoint contract, pagination behavior, prompt input, or schema changed.
- **Slice 5 follow-ups — the adopt path stops disagreeing with its own gate (frontend).** Two correctness fixes in code slice 5 widened exposure to, both flagged in the plan (§1.7, §8) rather than found late. **The confirm screen no longer re-fetches.** `DashboardStudyPlanSection` selected `publicPlans[0]` with **no qualifying predicate**, while onboarding gates the screen on `itemCount > 0 && readyCount > 0` — so the gate and the render could disagree, and a second fetch could return a different or newly-unqualified plan between the two. It now accepts the already-resolved, already-qualified plan and skips the lookup entirely, removing the disagreement by construction instead of duplicating the predicate in two places that can drift. **A zero-copy adopt is no longer presented as success.** `skippedCount` was surfaced as a notice but `copiedCount` was never checked, so an adopt that copied nothing routed the learner to an **empty collection** as though it had worked — at the most abandonment-sensitive moment, on the first thing they chose to do. It now reports that nothing was ready and keeps them where they are. Both were pre-existing; stage 3 opening practice-first to every profile type raised exposure roughly fourfold, which is the "a change increases exposure to pre-existing code" class `CLAUDE.md` requires a whole-release view to catch.
- **Slice 5, stage 3 — the Onboarding Intent Router (frontend).** Onboarding now asks *what would you like to do first* instead of assuming everyone authors a note. **Two equal-weight doors, neither ever disabled**: study with ready-made materials, or build from your own notes. When no Official Review Set resolves for the learner's program, option 1 carries a small neutral availability line rather than a badge or a disabled state — disabling it would make an ~18% path read as a dead end before the user has seen what is behind it, and its next screen offers genuinely useful alternatives. **The availability lookup fails open:** an unknown result renders *no* line at all, because telling a learner content does not exist when it does is the worse error. **The one structural eligibility change is opening practice-first beyond `BOARD_EXAM`** — a qualifying Review Set was previously unreachable for `STUDENT`, ~27% of profile-typed accounts. It is now gated on the chosen *intent* as well as availability, so someone who picked "own notes" is never shown the adopt screen just because a set happens to exist. **The unavailable state is a soft, explained re-route, not a rejection**: its primary action is the other intent, with Explore and a quiet "Finish setup" beneath, and nothing auto-redirects. Explore points at `/public/library?courseProgram=X`, deliberately not `/explore`, whose Review Sets tab ignores that parameter and reads the saved profile instead. **Completion now persists at each terminal exit, before `router.push`** — navigating first would leave a user who closes the tab mid-transition permanently un-onboarded — keeping the deferred-completion fallback and the once-per-mount StrictMode guard, both of which matter more with several exits than with one. `ONBOARDING_V2_COMPLETED` gains `intent` and `destination` **on the fallback exit only** — a correction the signoff audit forced: there are three emit sites, and the two *success* paths (Step 5 create completion, practice-first adopt) carry neither. `intent` stays inferable there from `method`; `destination` does not. So the outcome question is **not** yet answerable from a single event on the paths that matter most. Recorded rather than quietly widened; completing the field on all three exits is a `v0.71.1` candidate. Teacher copy is adapted without adding a second identity choice on top of Profile Type. Step 3's Back returns to the doors rather than skipping two screens to the learning profile.
- **Slice 5, stage 2 — onboarding persists what it collects, when it collects it (frontend).** `learnerLevel` and `courseProgram` were written only at **Step 5**, fire-and-forget with a swallowed error, so a failure lost them permanently and silently while `onboardingCompletedAt` was already set — the user was never routed back and had no idea anything was missing. **Five real accounts finished onboarding that way**, and `docs/features/onboarding.md` claimed the opposite ("saved before the user advances"), a line that has now finally been made true rather than corrected again. Both are now persisted at **Step 2 submit**, awaited, through the narrow `PUT /users/profile/learning-context` from stage 1, and a failure **blocks the step with a visible, retryable error** while the values are still on screen. `examDate` persists alongside them for `BOARD_EXAM`. Both Step 5 writes are removed rather than left as a fallback: a second writer would re-open the hole and could overwrite a value the user edited on the way back. **`profileType` persists at Step 1** so the server stops seeing null for the entire flow — deliberately *non*-blocking, because `completeOnboarding` re-sends it and nothing is at risk; the contrast with Step 2 is documented at both call sites so the asymmetry does not read as an oversight. The Review Set availability lookup keeps failing **open**: asserting "no Review Set" when one exists is the worse error. Also fixes pressure-test **L11** — Step 2's Course / Program now carries the required marker and helper text its sibling Learner Level already had. **No flow restructure**; the intent step is stage 3.
- **Slice 5, stage 1 — backend foundation for the Onboarding Intent Router (backend).** No user-visible change yet; this is what stages 2 and 3 write through. **New `PUT /users/profile/learning-context`** persists `learnerLevel` and `courseProgram` together, following the five existing single-purpose profile endpoints. Deliberately not the full-replace `PUT /users/profile`: that path is a read-modify-write requiring `firstName`, `email` and `username`, so onboarding would have to resend identity fields it has no business touching, risking a lost update against a concurrent edit and clobbering `pendingEmail` — and it cannot be called before those fields exist. Both fields are required together, because a partial write is exactly what produced the half-configured accounts the pressure test found. **`completeOnboarding` no longer nulls a previously-set exam date.** It wrote `resolveExamDate(request)` unconditionally, returning null for any non-`BOARD_EXAM` profile type, so completing onboarding as anything else silently destroyed a date the user had already given — undocumented and untested. That is not a tidy-up: `ROADMAP.md`'s target-habit definition segments retention on whether `examDate` is set and says so explicitly, *"not by profile_type… some non-BOARD_EXAM accounts may also set a real exam date"* — so the old behaviour destroyed the signal that metric reads. It also had to change before stage 2 persists the date at Step 2, or that write would be reverted at Step 5. Completion may now only ever **set** a date; clearing stays with the dedicated `PUT /users/profile/exam-date`, which owns that intent. **Three new analytics events** (`ONBOARDING_V2_INTENT_SELECTED`, `ONBOARDING_V2_INTENT_UNSUPPORTED_VIEWED`, `ONBOARDING_V2_FALLBACK_SELECTED`) added to the Java enum and mirrored in the frontend union, per the `CLAUDE.md` rule that the enum comes first. `ONBOARDING_V2_INPUT_METHOD_SELECTED` is retained and documented as a different signal — intent is *what do I want to do first*, input method is *generate from a topic vs paste my own*, now a sub-choice inside the create branch.
- **Inline fractions no longer render at script size in questions (frontend).** Reported from the public note page: the fraction in *"Which operation is most appropriate to simplify $\frac{x^3-4x^2+5x}{x-2}$?"* read far smaller than the words around it. The cause is KaTeX inline text style, which renders `\frac` numerator and denominator at roughly 0.7em — the math was never undersized as a whole (`.katex` is 1.21em by default and the app adds no override), only the fraction internals. Inline segments containing a size-collapsing construct — `\frac`, `\binom`, `\sum`, `\prod`, `\int`, `\oint`, `\lim` — are now promoted to `\displaystyle`, which restores full size while staying inline. Deliberately narrow: a blanket promotion would make every inline expression taller for no gain, and raising the KaTeX font size instead would oversize simple variables like `$x$` against body text. Applies wherever `renderMathText` is used, which since the C3 sweep is every quiz surface.
- **IDENTIFICATION quiz questions can no longer ask for notation the grader cannot match (backend + prompt).** Found in release testing. Challenge Quiz generated *"Identify the algebraic expression for the sum of the squares of two variables $x$ and $y$."* as an IDENTIFICATION question whose `acceptableAnswers` were `["sum of squares", "x squared plus y squared", "x squared plus y squared term"]` — so a learner typing `x^2 + y^2`, the answer the stem explicitly asks for and the one its own explanation gives, was marked **wrong**, while echoing the stem back was marked right. IDENTIFICATION is graded by exact normalized string equality with no maths-, chemistry- or code-aware comparison, so notation answers are ungradeable by construction: `x^2 + y^2`, `x² + y²` and `y² + x²` are all correct and none match as text. **Two independent generations produced the identical question** (different users, different study packs), so this is reproducible model behaviour rather than one bad sample; a survey of local data found **no** counter-example — every IDENTIFICATION question on hand was defective. Fixed on both sides: the Challenge Quiz prompt now forbids IDENTIFICATION whenever the answer is a symbolic form, states the rule for **every subject** rather than mathematics ("Identify the chemical formula for water" is equally invalid), keeps the valid case explicit (a formula's *name* is fine, the formula itself is not), and forbids restating the stem as an answer; and `QuizValidationUtils.isFormatStemMismatch` now rejects the question outright when the model ignores that instruction, which is deterministically tested where prompt behaviour cannot be. The guard is written against the **answer's form, not the subject**, with false-positive tests covering legitimate name/term identification. **Pre-existing data:** two rows already in `challenge_quiz_question_bank` carry the defective question; they are not migrated away, so the fix prevents new ones rather than repairing old ones.
- **Onboarding is completable by curator-role accounts — nobody curates during onboarding (backend).** Found in release testing, not by the pressure test. An **ADMIN** (or a `TEACHER` whose profile type was already persisted) taking any note-authoring path mid-onboarding hit the **curator** branch, which ignores the free-text `courseProgramText` entirely and demands `courseProgramIds` from a catalog picker that onboarding does not have — so both `POST /notes/generate` and `POST /notes` returned *"Choose at least one course or program."* and onboarding could not be finished. The B0 fix was correct but only reachable on the learner branch, so it never applied to these accounts. **Rule:** `NoteService.isTeacherSelectableOwner` and `NoteGenerationService.isCurator` both return false while `onboardingCompletedAt` is null, mirroring the exemption `OnboardingGuardService` already makes for mid-onboarding users. **This removes no authority** — an onboarded curator is unchanged, which two scope-guard tests assert directly so the fix cannot silently demote curators. The previous disposition of this as a near-zero-volume Known Limitation ("ADMIN accounts do not sign up") was wrong in the way that mattered: it reliably blocked the owner's own QA of onboarding. `NoteGenerationService` had **no curator-branch test at all**, which is why a change to that branch surfaced nothing; there are now four.
