- **C2/C5/C6/C7 fixed — the program axis stops lying to curators (backend + frontend).** Four defects that all shared a root shape: a surface reading the *learner* free-text program where the curator's authoritative value is the catalog selection, or validating a rule on the wrong side of the request. **C5, the headline UX fix of this release, was wrong in both directions** — the Note Editor sticky bar read `resolvedCourseProgram`, which for a curator is empty by design and falls back to the *profile* program, so it read "Tailored for: BSN" with **zero** programs selected while the "Add details" nag, gated on the same value, stayed hidden; both signals said a required field was done and Save then failed on exactly that field. Editing a curated multi-program note collapsed the bar to the subject alone for the same reason. Fixed by reusing the correct mapping that already existed 240 lines above in the same file, so the bar and the nag now share one value and cannot contradict each other. **C6:** Save was the only one of four surfaces not pre-validating the multi-program Domain Context rule, so a curator who used the family shortcut with "Add details" collapsed — the default — met a raw 400 naming a field inside the closed accordion; it now validates *and* opens the panel. **C7:** the admin Applicable Programs screen could reach the same invariant with no Domain Context control, and a failed save **reset the selection to the persisted set**, wiping every new pick so a curator who family-expanded had to re-choose all of them before retrying. The admin row now carries `domainContext` so the screen pre-validates and says where the field lives, and a failed save keeps the work. **C2:** the public Course / Program filter offered values that returned zero notes, because its personal-string half lacked the `NOT EXISTS` join guard that the facet counts already applied — still reachable after B2 via a curator editing programs on a note that retains a legacy string, which is why it was fixed rather than closed alongside it.
- **C3/C4 fixed — the LaTeX sweep is actually complete this time, and a lone `$` no longer breaks (frontend).** The rule this release already claimed ("any surface showing a question, option, or explanation routes through `renderMathText`") was **false when it was written** — the first pass fixed the components that happened to be open rather than a mapped inventory. Now covered: the shared `quiz-answer-review` **explanation**, one component behind five call sites, where the question directly above it rendered correctly so it read as a rendering bug rather than bad content; **options** on the unauthenticated `/quiz/[token]` page, the highest-visibility surface in the app; all four Interview Practice surfaces, which had zero coverage; the generated-quiz preview's Correct Answer echo, in a file the previous notes named as fixed; the matching-group option bank; Identification and Enumeration "Accepted answer(s)"; the public mini-quiz preview, an unauthenticated SEO surface; the onboarding quiz preview; and both demo pages. Completeness was verified by re-grepping for raw renders, not by walking the list. **C4:** a lone `$` is currency, not math — `"What is the cost of $5?"` produced two stray `<span>` wrappers, reintroducing the exact `break-words` displacement this release claims to have fixed, and `"Item A costs $5 and item B costs $10"` rendered the middle of the sentence as italic math with both dollar signs swallowed. Accountancy and Business Administration are seeded programs, so cost questions are routine. Root cause was that the guard and the tokenizer disagreed about what counts as math; they now share one scan, and `$` opens only when the next character is not whitespace and closes only when the previous one is not. **Correction to the finding:** it listed the Ask Companion guide FAQ as an in-scope miss; it is Q&A prose, not a quiz item, and was deliberately left alone along with the landing-page FAQ.
- **B2 fixed — a learner's program is never mechanically materialized into a catalog row (migration), and B1 fixed — `copyNote` no longer throws a foreign-key violation (backend).** `V107` backfills one join row per note whose program string matches the catalog with **no owner filter**, so from deploy day every learner who edited their Course / Program would have had a permanently inert edit — reads are join-first, a learner update never touches join rows, and the Applicable Programs endpoint is curator-gated, so they could not reach it. Nobody was affected: production never received the table, which is what made prevention possible instead of repair. **Ratified doctrine, now in `ADR-001` → *Representation authority*: a learner's personal free-text Course / Program must not be mechanically materialized into a catalog Applicable Program row.** Deliberately narrower than "learner-owned notes cannot carry join rows", which would contradict copy inheritance — read semantics never consult ownership, and a learner note served by the personal-string fallback is a canonical shape, not a degraded one. `V108` deletes rows whose note is both learner-owned and not a copy; curator-authored and copy-inherited rows survive. Not-a-copy is exact rather than a `created_at` heuristic, because copy inheritance is the only legitimate route to a join row on a learner note. **B1 folded in:** `copyNote` called `save()` then a raw-JDBC child insert with no `flush()` between, so it threw on any note carrying join rows — 18 of 40 public notes locally — breaking "Copy to my library" and silently emptying study-plan adoption. It was held behind B2 because the flush makes copy succeed and would have propagated the derived rows; after `V108` it propagates curator-authored and inherited rows only, which is the intent. Tests assert the flush **ordering**, not merely that it happens. The `V108` `DELETE` is kept outside the PL/pgSQL reporting block, with a structural test guarding that, because the block-stripping in the migration-test helper is exactly what left `V107`'s own backfill untested (finding B4). **Known limitation:** an ADMIN can curate another user's note through the shared endpoint, so rows an admin authored on a *non-copy learner-owned* note are removed by `V108`. Unreachable in production (no traffic between `V107` and `V108`) and deliberately not chased with a `source` provenance column.
- **C1 fixed — the multi-program invariant is enforced on the server, not just the client (backend).** `NoteService.update` validated the *request's* program set, but a learner's request carries no programs at all, so the count was always 0 and the rule was unenforceable on the only author who can reach it: a learner could copy a curated multi-program note and clear its Domain Context, producing exactly the state slice 4 forbids, with the client as the sole guard. Validation now reads the set that will be in effect *after* the update — the request for curators, whose request is the new set, and the stored join rows for learners, whose rows survive the update untouched. **The one-line fix recorded in the findings doc would have broken curators**, checking the pre-update set against the post-update domain: it blocks a legal reduction from several programs to one and lets an illegal one-to-many expansion through. Both directions are now covered by tests. `create` is unchanged and needs no stored-row lookup — the note does not exist yet. **No gate is owed.** A pre-existing violating population would have locked its owner out of every later save, since Domain Context is curator-only — but it cannot exist: production has not received `note_course_program` at all, slices 1–4 deploy as one unit, V107 creates at most one row per note, and both the slice 4 invariant and this fix ship in that same deploy. `docs/claude-plans/c1-multi-program-null-domain-sizing.sql` is retained as a **post-deploy** check, where a non-zero result would mean a write path regressed rather than legacy data.
- **B0 fixed — new users can complete onboarding again (frontend).** Onboarding collected a Course / Program at Step 2 but sent it on neither of its Step 3 note calls, while the profile value it would have fallen back to is not persisted until Step 5. Every new user on the create-note path was stopped by *"Choose at least one course or program."* on a screen that has no such field, and could not reach the dashboard. Both calls now send it — `courseProgramText` on `createNote`, and the program argument on `generateNoteFromTopic` — which unblocks the generate path at *Generate* and both paths at *Generate Study Pack*. Regression coverage added on both sides: the onboarding test asserts both call payloads exactly, and two backend tests cover the shape where the request supplies the program and the profile has none, which no existing course/program test did. Rule recorded in `docs/features/onboarding.md`. **The ADMIN variant is now fixed too — see the bullet above.**
- **LaTeX now renders in quiz questions, options, and explanations (frontend).** A generated Algebra question read literally as `simplify \(\frac{x^3 - 4x^2 + 5x}{x - 2}\)?` in Quick Review. KaTeX and a text-plus-math renderer already existed in this repo, but had only ever been wired to *working solutions*. **Unrelated to Applicable Programs** — found while reviewing this release, fixed here. The first pass covered only the components it happened to open; the result and preview surfaces (`practice-quiz-card`, `generated-quiz-preview-page-client`, `quiz-matching-group`) render question text directly and were still broken, and **explanations were raw on every surface**, including session pages whose question text was already correct. `renderMathText` returns the raw string untouched when text contains no math delimiters, so the DOM is unchanged for the overwhelming majority of questions — wrapping every string had silently moved `break-words` off the elements callers style. Rule recorded in `docs/features/quiz.md`: any surface showing a question, option, or explanation routes through `renderMathText`, and a raw `{item.question}` is the bug.
- **Note authoring UX pass (frontend).** Four fixes from real use of the slice 4 editor. **Course / Program(s) keeps its original position in the main metadata grid for curators** — slice 4 had moved only the curator control into the gated "Generation & discovery" fieldset, which misfiled a discovery field as generation metadata and contradicted its own helper text. **A curator's programs pre-fill from their profile again** on a new note; slice 4 seeded only the learner free-text draft, so curators saw "No course programs selected" while the footer read "Tailored for: Accountancy". The pre-fill is guarded three ways — never in edit mode, never after the author touches the selection, and silently skipped when the profile value has no catalog match. **Subject is surfaced through the always-visible sticky bar** (`Tailored for: Accountancy · Algebra`, or `· no subject yet`) rather than by moving it up the form, so authors learn it exists and is editable while "Add details" stays collapsed and capture-first is preserved; no AI call is added to the editor, so subject suggestions remain post-generation. **The collapsed "Add details" summary reflects state instead of restating rules** — it previously read "Course / Program is required", which after the pre-fill usually described something already done. Field order stays Title → Subject → Course / Program(s), the sequence students actually author in.
- **Single Course / Program(s) axis (slice 4).** Removed derived-primary synchronization and the `NoteApplicableProgramsMaintenanceService`; curator saves reconcile catalog joins atomically while learner authoring continues to write its single free-text personal program. The resolver uses Domain Context, then exactly one joined catalog name, then the personal note string, then the profile string. Private and public note detail now say `Applicable to N programs` and expose the program list. **Two authoring modes on one gating line, ratified in `ADR-001`:** curators (Teacher/Admin) get catalog-only programs and one-or-many; learners get free text and exactly one. This is a product distinction — multi-program authoring is a curation act — not a permission tier, and `notes.course_program` is the **personal-notes program field** rather than a legacy column. Domain Context becomes required above one program, enforced on both save endpoints, because the prompt's domain constraint is unsatisfiable given several disciplines.
- **Product vision ratified (docs).** NoteLib is **a learning system built on top of a knowledge library** — the library is the foundation, the learning journey is the product. Three layers earned strictly in order: Trust (comprehensive Official Review Sets) → Habit (Study Packs, Companion, Progress, Review Sets) → Community (user-created knowledge). **Community content is no longer the primary acquisition strategy**; the community vision is not abandoned, it is being earned in the correct order. Recorded in `docs/product/SPEC.md` → Product Philosophy and as a governing block in the ROADMAP's prioritization lens, because it changes what outranks what when scoping.

- **Applicable Programs UX polish (pre-signoff pass).** Cards now state reach as a summary instead of a list — one program is named, several render as `Applies to N programs` — and the identity row above the title is the Subject badge alone. Listing names there made them read as a second identity, left adjacent names with no delimiter (`Accountancy Architecture` scanned as one value), and mostly repeated what a program-filtered learner had already asked for. On Note Detail the header collapses both program facts to one muted line — `Accountancy · Applies to 6 programs` — with the count suppressed at a single applicable program, since the seeded default is exactly one. A first attempt rendered the full set as pills in a bordered block; that cost roughly a quarter of the mobile viewport and pushed `Start Quick Review` and the whole content tab bar below the fold, and it boxed a single line for plain student owners who never see Applicable Programs at all. Names now appear only where they can be acted on — the Edit details panel and the admin table — which makes one rule across every surface: **names where you author, counts where you read.** Authoring keeps both fields but explains why the first seeds the second, which is the point authors reliably misread as duplication. Domain Context and Note Learner Level empty options move off implementation vocabulary — "Use … fallback" becomes "Automatic — based on the program / reader" — and the fieldset is now "Generation & discovery". Copy and presentation only; no resolution, persistence, or query behavior changed.
- **Slice 3 — Program Family authoring shortcut.** The shared Applicable Programs control on Note Editor, Note Detail, and the Admin Dashboard now derives Program Families from the existing catalog and can add every currently-unselected member in one action. Expansion unions into the explicit selection, shows removable chips immediately for trimming, and is unconditional across Subject, Domain Context, and learner level; it adds no network call or persisted family state, never expands at read time, and ships with no backend, schema, endpoint, or catalog change.
- **Slice 2 — Applicable Programs discovery reads.** Private Library course/program filters and facets and Public Library slug filters and search now read `note_course_program` first, while notes with no join rows retain their legacy `notes.course_program` filter, facet, search, and shareable-URL behavior. Paginated list projections aggregate joined catalog names in the list query so cards can render program information without an N+1 request (the card's own presentation was then reworked by the UX polish bullet above); facets use the mutually-exclusive join/legacy `UNION ALL`, so multi-program counts may correctly exceed the note total and both filter panels explain why. `GET /course-programs` keeps its existing path and `List<String>` contract but now includes join-only program names, intentionally widening both discovery and the legacy authoring suggestions that consume the same endpoint. Program Families remain unexpanded and Applicable Programs remain prompt-free.
- **Slice 1 — Applicable Programs storage and write surfaces.** V107 adds the unmapped JDBC-only `note_course_program` join with uniqueness, lookup indexes, note-delete cascade, and a one-row-per-resolvable-note backfill driven from the live `notes.course_program` string (exact match plus the sole `Bsed` → `Education` alias); excluded strings remain unchanged and receive no row. Authenticated catalog and owner-safe note APIs now support full-set reconciliation for Teacher/Admin authors, note create/update keeps an untouched *derived* set aligned with the legacy string — including the empty set, so a note that gains a catalog-resolvable course/program after creation gains its join row — while preserving curated sets, and the Note Editor, Note Detail, and a paginated Admin Dashboard table expose one shared catalog-backed multi-select. This slice remains additive: filters, facets, badges, search, and URLs still read only the legacy string and nothing learner-facing reads the join.

## v0.70.0 - Canonical Knowledge Completion

**Status: Released**

Theme: finish what `v0.69.0` started — make the canonical authoring axes correctable after generation, give programs a real catalog, and stop question pools being invalidated by whoever happens to be reading.

### Planned Scope

- **Authoring metadata editable on `STUDY_PACK_READY` notes (frontend).** The note editor closes once a Study Pack exists, so Domain Context and Note Learner Level cannot be corrected after generation from any surface the UI offers. Content stays locked (that lock exists to keep the note consistent with its pack); these two fields do not touch the existing pack and only shape *future* generation. **This is first because it blocks R4 step 1**, which asks for an existing note with a generated pack to be re-set and regenerated. Implementation found the gate is entirely client-side and that this item and the one below are the same change — see Shipped.
- **Note detail page's inline "Edit details" panel exposes both axes (frontend).** That panel duplicates Subject, Course/Program, Audience and Tags but omits the two axes `v0.69.0` shipped, so it reads as a complete authoring surface while silently missing them. It cost two people real time in the first hour of production use.
- **`course_programs` catalog + `program_families` (backend, data).** Deferred from `v0.69.0`. Two production reads now done: `15-vocabulary-and-impact-results.md` Parts 1 and 3. **32 values, zero character-level collisions across all three sources** — the second read (`16-program-vocabulary-seed-followup.sql`) added `note_collections.course_program` (`V76`), which `11` never covered, and confirmed collections contribute no values of their own. Resolves to **21 seeded programs, 11 excluded**, plus one `program_families` row. Nullable FK on `notes` and `users` **alongside** the existing string columns; nothing reads the FK. Retires `ExamGoalConfig`/`exam-hub-config.ts`'s hand-synced name lists.
- **Question pool / bank learner-level re-keying (backend).** Deferred from `v0.69.0`; production read now done. `exam_question_pool.learner_level` and `challenge_quiz_question_bank.learner_level` move off the **reader's** level onto the **note's**, so a College note's pool is no longer discarded the moment a Grade School user touches it. **No pre-stamping migration** — measured at 57 of 1,244 READY pools (4.6%) and 5 of 6,235 bank rows, a lazy regeneration spread across normal traffic. `PostSessionNextStepService` must be threaded in the **same** PR as the five `ChallengeQuizService` write sites.
- **`10-high-school-classification.sql` cleanup (docs).** It still says V104 where V105 applied, "expected 11 rows" where one was deleted as a duplicate, and describes clearing `course_program` — a step ADR-001's second corollary deferred out of the release entirely.

Anti-drift — what this release explicitly does **NOT** change:

- **No Domain Context backfill onto pool/bank rows (PR 6b).** Gated on R4: a changed value set would invalidate it. Only the *learner level* re-keying ships here.
- **No `note_course_program` and no Applicable Programs.** Still Release B. **`notes.course_program_id` and `users.course_program_id` are written and read by nothing** — no filter, facet, badge, search predicate, or URL changes. The one sanctioned read of the new tables is the Exam Hub name lookup described under Resolved decisions: it reads the `course_programs` **table by name**, never either FK, and must be behavior-preserving. Anything beyond that is Release B.
- **No authoring-by-inference implementation.** ADR-001 records it as a direction, explicitly gated behind R4. Pre-filling from a Review Set is *not* in this release.
- **The note-level learner depth axis is not removed, narrowed, or renamed before R4 runs** — owner constraint, 2026-08-04.
- **Note content stays locked after `STUDY_PACK_READY`.** Only the two authoring axes become editable; this is not a reopening of the content lock.
- **`docs/product/EXAM_MODES.md` is untouched** — locked contract, exactly 5 modes.

### Resolved decisions — PR 5 is scopeable

Four owner rulings, none of which PR 5 may re-litigate. The full 32-row seed table and the 21/11 split live in `15-vocabulary-and-impact-results.md` Part 3.

**1. Computer Science / Information Technology / Software Engineering — ruled by the owner 2026-08-04.** `08` ruled all three survive as distinct programs; the production read disagreed — IT has 74 notes across 11 subjects, Software Engineering has 4 notes whose only subject is *Computer Science*, and Computer Science has **0 notes** against 3 user profiles.

**Ruling: seed `Information Technology` as a catalog program. `Computer Science` and `Software Engineering` stay user-side values with a NULL catalog FK**, pending a curator ruling if either grows real curriculum. Nothing is deleted — the 4 Software Engineering notes and the 3 Computer Science profiles keep their existing strings, exactly as the catalog's other unmappable values do. This reverses part of `08` on evidence `08` did not have; it is ratified rather than assumed.

**2. The three `Senior High – …` strands are seeded — ruled by the owner 2026-08-04.** Closes the call `11`'s Query D deferred to PR 5. STEM (4 notes), ABM (4), and HUMSS (3) are curriculum *tracks*, not bare levels: `V105` deliberately left their `domain_context` NULL so the strand keeps reaching the prompt as the effective authoring domain, and excluding them would leave the value that actually reaches generation sitting outside the catalog. `Grade School`, `Junior High`, and `High School` stay excluded as bare levels now fully described by `learner_level` + `domain_context`. Excluding a value never means clearing it — ADR-001's second corollary still defers that out of the release entirely.

**3. `Medical – Surgical Nursing` is dropped from PNLE — ruled by the owner 2026-08-04.** The second production read found it matches **0 notes, 0 users, and 0 collections**: it exists only in `ExamGoalConfig:15` and `frontend/lib/exam-hub-config.ts:26`, under a comment in both files asserting that course/program values "must match production DB values exactly." It never has. It is also a PNLE board *subject area* rather than a degree program, failing the catalog's classification rule on the same grounds as `Biology`. **PNLE maps to `Nursing` only.** Behavior-preserving in effect — the dropped value matched nothing — but it must be proven by test rather than asserted. The Exam Hub's multi-program dedupe path stays; PNLE simply stops exercising it. `RELEASES.md:253`'s worked example of that dedupe is left as the historical record it is.

**4. `Bsed` → `Education` is the only non-exact FK mapping.** An enumerated single-row alias sanctioned by `11`'s settled judgment calls, written as a literal exception and never generalised into a normalization rule. The stored string stays `Bsed`.

### Verification — R4 RESOLVED 2026-08-04, after deploy. Bulk authoring is unblocked.

**R4 carried over from `v0.69.0` as a `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-08-18]` and was run against production on 2026-08-04, once this release deployed.** The first scope item above is what made it runnable: both authoring axes became correctable on a note that already has a Study Pack. Full runbook, note selection, and the five-point scoring rubric: `docs/claude-prompt/canonical-knowledge-architecture-out/17-r4-verification-runbook.md`.

**Passed on all three steps. The ratified 8-value Domain Context set is not amended.**

1. **Control** — a Civil Engineering note set to `Civil Engineering` + `Board Exam Review` regenerated near-identically, slightly richer. No wiring regression.
2. **The actual test** — a `Strength of Materials` note set to the deliberately broader `Engineering Sciences` kept every domain marker: `hoop (circumferential)` / `longitudinal` stress, `Lame's equations`, the `ASME Boiler and Pressure Vessel Code`, the thin/thick-wall classification, and its civil-engineering application framing. **Zero of five drift checks fired.** Confirmed in production afterwards that only `domain_context` changed and `learner_level` stayed NULL, so it is a clean single-variable result.
3. **Level precedence** — a `BOARD_EXAM_REVIEW` note read by a `COLLEGE` profile produced board-level questions (plausible-distractor framings, and a four-step trapezoid→wetted-perimeter→hydraulic-radius→Manning's problem rather than a single plug-in), scored against `docs/features/challenge-quiz.md`'s own level rubric. ADR-001 rule 3 held.

**The bulk-authoring block is lifted.** That sentence was load-bearing across three releases; it is satisfied, not merely restated.

**What it does not settle.** R4 validates the Domain Context *value set*. The **applicability groupings** in `06`/`08` remain unverified against current PRC board syllabi — a curator question, and precisely what Release B's family-expansion defaults depend on. Do not read "R4 passed" as "applicability is settled."

**One incidental finding, not drift** — the regenerated `Strength of Materials` summary dropped a comparison table and a Common Misconceptions block its prior version had, while the surviving prose stayed fully domain-specific. A level-signal explanation is falsified by the note's own history (the original pack also had `learner_level` NULL and *did* carry the table). Logged as one-sample structural variance with its own ROADMAP Backlog Index row, on the user-facing grounds that a regeneration can silently drop content a learner valued.

### Shipped

- **Pre-signoff pressure-test fixes (backend, data, frontend).** The full release-wide pressure test `CLAUDE.md` requires (four inventory agents synthesised and pressure-tested through `advisor`) found four defects no per-PR audit could see; all are fixed here. **(1)** `ExamGoalCourseProgramProvider` memoised its fail-open fallback permanently — `computeIfAbsent` cached whatever the loader returned, so a single transient DB failure, or one request arriving before `V106` applied, pinned the hardcoded literal lists for the JVM's lifetime. A curator's catalog edit would then stay invisible until redeploy, defeating the very retirement this release claims, and the damage reached past the Exam Hub to `ProgressReportService` and `PostSessionNextStepService`, which share the singleton. Only a successful non-empty catalog read is memoised now, and the load moved outside the map so a blocking JDBC call no longer holds a `ConcurrentHashMap` bin monitor. Two new tests pin recovery after a throw and after an empty read — the pre-existing fallback tests each called the provider exactly once and so could not have caught a sticky fallback. **(2)** `NoteService.update` discarded the requested Target Audience for non-Teacher owners and re-derived it from the owner's **current** profile, so an unrelated metadata save — a title fix from the inline panel this release makes the sanctioned surface for `STUDY_PACK_READY` notes — silently rewrote `notes.target_profile_type`, a live Public Library discovery filter, and discarded a copied note's authored audience. The stored value is now preserved, with the profile mapping retained only for legacy rows that carry none. Pre-existing, but this release increased exposure to it. **(3)** `V106` gained `ck_course_programs_exam_goal_slug` per the repo's enum-column convention, so a curator typo such as `'LET'` is rejected at write time instead of silently becoming a row the provider cannot see and misreporting as an empty catalog; plus a non-fatal `RAISE NOTICE` of per-table backfill counts following `V105`'s precedent, so a zero-match backfill leaves deploy-log evidence instead of reporting success — the silent no-op mode `V104` names. Amending `V106` rather than adding a `V107` was verified safe: it is absent from `flyway_schema_history` in every environment, including local. **(4)** Note Detail's **Domain Context** helper text no longer promises that a correction "applies to the next generation" — true for Note Learner Level, false for Domain Context, since neither the pool nor the bank carries a domain column and a `READY` pool short-circuits generation entirely. Verified on real Postgres 16 in `BEGIN`/`ROLLBACK` (21 programs seeded, 37 notes + 1 user backfilled, `NOTICE` emitted, bad slug rejected); backend 1377 tests and frontend 164 suites / 1663 tests green.
- **Planning-doc corrections that were themselves blocking work (docs).** `AGENTS.md:1062` still said a `STUDY_PACK_READY` note "allows only title/courseProgram/subject/tags" — stale because of this release's own first item, and enough to make a Codex prompt refuse to expose the two axes the inline panel now has. The Learning Profile Metadata and LLM Context Builder blocks gained the ADR-001 supersession header the Course/Program block already had, with both resolution chains corrected. `10-high-school-classification.sql` had V104 where V105 applied, "expected 11 rows" where production shows 10 (one deleted as a duplicate in between), and described clearing `course_program` — a step ADR-001's second corollary deferred out of the release entirely.
- **Audited course/program catalog and Exam Hub program-source retirement.** V106 creates `course_programs` with exactly **21** curator-approved programs and `program_families` with the `Engineering` family, adds nullable `notes.course_program_id` / `users.course_program_id`, and backfills exact string matches without changing any legacy string. The sole literal exception is `Bsed` -> the `Education` FK while the stored `Bsed` value remains untouched. Explicit catalog exclusions are `Junior High`, `High School`, `Grade School` (bare levels); `Civil Service`, `Professional / Board Exam Review`, `Self Study / Personal Learning` (goals/activities); `Engineering` (a family); `Biology` (a subject); `Software Engineering` and `Computer Science` (owner-ratified contested computing values); and `Bsed` (an alias, not a separate program). `Medical – Surgical Nursing`, a config-only PNLE subject area with zero production matches, is also removed; PNLE now maps to `Nursing` only. Exam Hub program names resolve from `course_programs.exam_goal_slug` through a cached provider and public server-render endpoint, with the literal lists retained only as fail-open fallbacks. The generic multi-program dedupe path remains even though PNLE no longer exercises it. Tests pin the 21/1 seed, all four U+2013 values, exact/alias/null-FK behavior, catalog/fallback drift, and the unchanged PNLE note/set result when the zero-match alias is removed.
- **Question pools and the Challenge bank now key learner depth to the note's effective curriculum level.** Single-note Long Exam and Board Exam pools, ordinary Challenge claims, Redo Missed claims/counts, generated bank rows, and Official-template copies all resolve `note learner level -> reader learner level -> COLLEGE` through `StudyPackGenerationContextResolver`. Reader-level changes no longer invalidate content authored for a level carried by the note; notes without an authored level retain the previous reader-level behavior **except where the reader's own profile level is also null** — see Known Limitations. Existing rows are preserved with no migration or pre-stamp: the measured one-time mismatch wave is **57 of 1,244 READY pools (4.6%) and 5 of 6,235 bank rows (0.08%)**, plus a further **40 READY pools and 15 bank rows** in the null-by-null cohort the original impact read never compared (measured 2026-08-04 during the pre-commit audit; the two sets may overlap, so they are deliberately not summed). Pools refresh and bank shortfalls are filled lazily through normal traffic rather than bulk-regenerated.
- **`AGENTS.md`'s Learner Level anti-drift block retired its two ADR-superseded rules.** Found while auditing the re-keying diff. The block still instructed the next session to level static content by course/program and to **"never reintroduce per-note learner level columns"** — the second being a direct prohibition on `notes.learner_level`, which `v0.69.0` had already shipped. `v0.69.0` added a supersession note to the Course/Program block (`:608`) but not to this one, so a Codex prompt reading it would have refused to touch the very axis this release is built on. Now carries the same supersession header, corrects the resolution chains to `effectiveAuthoringDomain()` / `effectiveCurriculumLevel()`, states the curriculum-floor rule explicitly, and keeps the one clause the ADR did **not** retire — that shared content must never be calibrated from the *reader's* level, which is the `buildSubjectSuggestionGuidanceBlock` defect `v0.69.0`'s pressure test caught.
- **Domain Context and Note Learner Level are correctable after generation (frontend).** Note Detail's inline metadata panel — the only editing surface a `STUDY_PACK_READY` note offers from the UI — now exposes both axes to Teacher/Admin authors, behind the same `isTeacherSelectableNoteTarget` gate the Note Editor uses, with the same option lists, the same blank-means-fallback semantics, and the Note Editor's subject-equals-Domain-Context advisory ported alongside. Helper text states that a correction shapes the *next* generation and leaves the existing Study Pack alone. Unblocks R4 step 1 for anyone working from the UI rather than a hand-typed editor URL.
- **Silent-null guard on the inline save path.** `PUT /notes/{id}` is a full replace and the two selects only render for Teacher/Admin, so a plain user saving tags would have sent an empty draft value and nulled a set Domain Context. Both axes now resolve through the same `canEditAuthoringMetadata ? draft : note` ternary the audience field already used, covered by a test asserting a non-Teacher save round-trips both axes unchanged.
- **Draft seeding consolidated into `toMetadataDraft`.** The inline draft was rebuilt field-by-field in five places (load sync, `?edit=1` auto-open, `Edit`, `Cancel`, post-save reset); the fifth was not in the original scoping. One helper now owns it, so the next field added cannot be wired into four sites and blank in the fifth.
- **Scope correction — the lock was never a backend one.** `NoteService.update` (`:185-211`) has no status guard and already persisted both axes on a ready note; `note-editor-page-client.tsx` has no ready guard either (`handleSave:733`, `disableContentEditing={false}`). The only gate was `private-note-detail-page-client.tsx:1329`, where `handleEdit` routes ready notes into the inline panel instead of `/notes/{id}/edit`. So a mis-set axis was **not** permanent — it was correctable by typing the editor URL, which is how R4 step 1 became runnable ahead of this work. That route stays deliberately unguarded: guarding it would reopen the content lock and remove the escape hatch. Recorded in `docs/features/notes.md` rather than silently fixed, and the first Planned Scope bullet corrected from "backend + frontend" to "frontend".

### Known Limitations

- **Correcting a note's learner level orphans that note's existing Challenge bank rows, and the bank's duplicate-key hazard is not actually best-effort.** Found by the pool/bank re-keying audit as an interaction between two items in *this* release, which no per-PR review could see: the re-keying makes bank claims key on the note's effective curriculum level, and the editable-authoring-axes item makes that level correctable after generation. Every post-generation level correction therefore leaves that note's whole bank stranded at the old level — unclaimable, never re-stamped, and refilled by fresh LLM generation. Rows are preserved rather than deleted (ADR-001 rule 2 holds) and the measured population today is 5 of 6,235, but it is no longer bounded by that figure once authoring corrections become routine. **The sharper edge is the write path.** `uq_challenge_quiz_question_bank_user_pack_key` covers `(user_id, study_pack_id, question_key)` and **not** `learner_level`, so a stranded row's key is absent from `disallowedQuestionKeys` (only *claimed* rows are passed to the model) and the LLM can regenerate it — a duplicate insert. `ChallengeQuizQuestionBankService.persistGeneratedQuestions:118` batches a `saveAll` under a broad `catch (RuntimeException)`, but with an assigned UUID `@Id` and no `@Version` the INSERT flushes at commit, **outside** that catch and inside `startChallengeQuiz`'s `@Transactional` — so `docs/features/challenge-quiz.md`'s "bank persistence is best-effort and never blocks a Challenge session" is already false for constraint violations. This is pre-existing (a row claimed by another live session is likewise excluded from `findClaimableForUpdate` today) and was deliberately **not** fixed here rather than widening a re-keying PR into the bank's write path. The fix when it is scoped is `copyTemplateQuestions`'s existing shape: per-row saves with a `DataIntegrityViolationException` catch, which is itself the evidence that this collision class is real and was already known at one of the two bank write sites.
- **Unstamped pools and bank rows owned by a null-profile-level user change behavior, and were outside the impact read.** `sameLearnerLevel` previously received the raw nullable reader level, so a `NULL` pool level matched a `NULL` user level and sampled without refreshing; `effectiveCurriculumLevel` never returns null, so that pairing now mismatches and takes the lazy refresh path. `findClaimableForUpdate`'s `(:learnerLevel is null and question.learnerLevel is null)` branch is dead for the same reason. Pools self-heal — one refresh stamps them `COLLEGE` and they match thereafter — but bank rows do not, so a null-level user's null-stamped rows are permanently unclaimable. `12-pool-bank-relevel-impact.sql` compared note level against reader level and never covered the null-by-null cell, so this cohort is **not** inside the 57 / 5 figures above. Learner level is required from onboarding onward but remains nullable in storage for pre-existing accounts (`AGENTS.md:952`), and the cohort is **not** empty: measured 2026-08-04, **40 READY pools and 15 bank rows**. The 40 pools cost one extra lazy regeneration each and then match permanently; the 15 bank rows are the lasting item, and they are the same orphan class as the bullet above. Neither is destructive and neither was judged worth a pre-stamping migration this release, but the figure belongs on the record rather than inside a query nobody re-runs.

## v0.69.0 - Canonical Knowledge Foundation

**Status: Released**

Theme: stop the library from being forced to duplicate shared knowledge. One canonical note can now be authored for a body of knowledge that many programs share — an Algebra note authored once for Engineering Mathematics, rather than eleven near-identical copies, one per engineering program.

This is **Release A of `docs/architecture/ADR-001-canonical-knowledge-architecture.md`** (ratified 2026-08-03). Full planning: `docs/claude-prompt/canonical-knowledge-architecture-out/` — `01` (critique, code audit, 14-item migration inventory), `05` (production vocabulary audit + baselines), `06`/`08` (the Domain Context taxonomy and its Query K validation).

**Why now.** Authoring of the Civil Engineering Review Set's Engineering Mathematics subject plan was deliberately stopped: creating those Algebra topic notes under a one-program-per-note model would have committed the library to duplicating them for ten further engineering programs, along with their Study Packs, question pools, flashcards, memorization decks, and all future maintenance. The duplication has already begun at n=2 — `Strength of Materials` carries "Stress and Strain in Strength of Materials" (Civil Engineering) and "Stress, Strain, and Material Strength" (Mechanical Engineering), the same knowledge as two notes.

**Success metric** (ratified wording): *eliminates duplication of shared knowledge as NoteLib expands to more programs.* Comprehensive Review Sets, faster curriculum expansion, and lower maintenance are downstream benefits, not the architectural guarantee — this release removes the *multiplier* on every program after the first; it does not reduce the ~60% of any program's content that is program-specific, and it does not change assembly.

### Planned Scope

- **`notes.domain_context` (backend + prompts + admin frontend).** New nullable column carrying a curated, closed 8-value set: `Engineering Mathematics`, `Engineering Sciences`, `Civil Engineering`, `Professional Practice & Regulation`, `General Education`, `Professional Education`, `Nursing`, `Accountancy`. Replaces `course_program` as the LLM's authoritative domain constraint. This is the load-bearing change: before PR 2, `OpenAiLlmStudyPackService` instructed the model to *"treat the course/program above as the authoritative academic domain … Do not blend in material from unrelated disciplines"* — an instruction that is logically unsatisfiable once a note is applicable to eleven programs. Touches the resolver (`StudyPackGenerationContextResolver`), `StudyPackGenerationContext`, the Java-assembled note/Study Pack context blocks, subject guidance, quantitative detection, admin note authoring, and Bulk Generate. The Companion `{COURSE_PROGRAM}` placeholder remains the Review Set's independent curation label. Normal users never see Domain Context — their notes resolve through the fallback chain `domain_context` → `course_program` → user's `course_program`.
- **[DEFERRED to v0.70.0] `course_programs` catalog + `program_families` (backend, data).** Not shipped in this release. It is fully independent of everything above and blocked only on one production read — the exact 32 program strings, which `05-vocabulary-results.md` never enumerates (the 5 user-side-only values are counted but unnamed, and prose loses exact bytes such as the U+2013 in the Senior High labels). Query written and verified: `11-program-vocabulary-seed.sql`. The production audit found only 32 distinct program values across notes and users with **zero** character-level collisions, so this is a curated seed plus roughly six semantic judgment calls (`Bsed`→Education, the Computer Science/IT/Software Engineering overlap, `Engineering` as a family, `Biology` as a subject, and `Civil Service`/`Professional / Board Exam Review`/`Self Study / Personal Learning` as goals rather than programs). Nullable FK on `notes` and `users` **alongside** the existing string columns; nothing reads the FK yet. Retires `ExamGoalConfig`/`frontend/lib/exam-hub-config.ts`'s hand-synced program-name lists and their documented en-dash fragility.
- **`notes.learner_level` (backend + prompts).** New nullable column for the depth a note was authored at, outranking the user's own level. Fixes a live generation bug rather than adding a nicety: **49 notes currently hold a K-12 grade level in `course_program`** (`Junior High` 24, `High School` 11, `Grade School` 3, `Senior High – STEM` 4, `– ABM` 4, `– HUMSS` 3), which feeds a grade level straight into the authoritative-academic-domain prompt line. Backfill is mechanical for five of those six values, and all 49 are in zero collections. **`High School` (11 notes) is the exception** — ADR-001's Legacy-data policy forbids a blanket mapping, so it is a per-note classification pass from actual content (review query: `10-high-school-classification.sql`, run against production), and notes that cannot be classified confidently keep `learner_level`, `domain_context`, *and* `course_program` as they are rather than gaining a Domain Context that would evict their only remaining level signal. **The backfill split into PR 4a (the 27 pure-level notes) and PR 4b (the remaining 22 after the curator mapping and strand decision), and neither clears `course_program`** — clearing is cosmetic once a Domain Context is set, is irreversible, and would activate a live editor defect that silently submits the editing admin's own profile program for a note whose program is null (`note-editor-page-client.tsx:269`/`:592`/`:620`). Whether `Grade School`/`Junior High` survive as program values is PR 5's call, alongside the other non-program values it already excludes.
- **[DEFERRED to v0.70.0] Question pool / bank re-keying (backend).** Not shipped in this release. Blocked on two things: a production read sizing the refresh wave (`12-pool-bank-relevel-impact.sql` — changing the comparison makes existing pools mismatch and hit `refreshPool`, which regenerates via the LLM, and ADR-001 rule 2 forbids destructive regeneration), and on R4, because its Domain Context backfill onto pool rows would be invalidated by a changed value set. Scoping found a divergence `09` never mentions: five bank call sites in `ChallengeQuizService` have a generation context in scope, but `PostSessionNextStepService:80` passes the reader's level into `countEligibleIncorrectQuestions` and never touches the resolver — fixing only the write sites would make the Redo Missed Questions availability check and the claim query disagree. Original scope, unchanged: `exam_question_pool.learner_level` and `challenge_quiz_question_bank.learner_level` are written from the **user's** level today, and `ExamQuestionPoolService.sameLearnerLevel()` (`:368-374`, called at `:101`) invalidates a pool when the current user's level differs. Both move to the note's level, otherwise a College note's pool would be discarded the moment a Grade School user touched it. Includes an explicit policy for existing rows.
- **Subject-equals-context admin nudge (frontend).** ~334 notes carry a program name as their `subject` (`Professional Education` 250, `Nursing` 62, `Engineering Mathematics` 10, `Accountancy` 8, `Architecture` 3), already violating `buildSubjectSuggestionGuidanceBlock`'s own rule against overly broad subjects. When a note's `subject` equals its `domain_context`, warn the admin that the subject is probably too broad — a nudge, never a hard validation error.
- **Documentation corrections folded in (docs only).** `AGENTS.md`'s Course/Program UI rules currently state that `courseProgram` is "the top-level note-classification shelf" and that a `course_programs` table must not be added — both now contradict ADR-001 and would mislead the next session or Codex prompt. `CLAUDE.md`'s source-of-truth list omits `docs/architecture/` entirely, so nothing points a fresh session at the binding architecture record.

Anti-drift — what this release explicitly does **NOT** change:

- **No `note_course_program` join table, and no Applicable Programs.** That is Release B: multi-release, irreversible once filters and badges read the join, and it fails the ROADMAP bootstrap test's clause 2 as a knowing commitment. Nothing in this release touches the private Library filter, the Public Library filter or search predicate, the facet-count queries, note card badges, or any URL. Public Library stays subject-keyed at `/public/library/[subject]/[slug]`.
- **`Architecture` deliberately gets no Domain Context**, despite 837 notes — the second-largest program in the library. It uses the program-name fallback until shared canonical knowledge accumulates, because duplication reduction and not note volume is what earns a context. Thin programs (Law, Medicine, Criminology, Psychology, Aviation, Business Administration, Physical Therapy, Civil Service, Pharmacy, Information Technology) are **not** added to the curated set either — `domain_context IS NULL` is the promotion marker, and the promotion backlog stays a one-line query.
- **No regeneration of existing Study Packs.** Every pack generated before this release carries no learner-level signal at all; that gap closes only by regeneration, which stays explicitly user-confirmed per the versioning rule. Pre-existing packs remain level-signal-free, documented rather than silently inconsistent.
- **`notes.target_profile_type` is unchanged.** Verified to be a live Public Library audience filter (`PublicLibraryRepositoryImpl:176-178`, `NoteController:594`/`:636`), so it sits on the discovery axis and does not conflict with the new learner level. Whether precise program facets make it redundant is judged at the end of Release B.
- **Review Set composition is unchanged.** Review Sets keep composing notes by explicit reference with no program constraint; a Review Set's own course/program stays a curation label, never derived from and never validated against its notes.
- **`docs/product/EXAM_MODES.md` is untouched** — locked contract, exactly 5 modes, no mode structure changes here.
- **Applicability defaults are out of scope and remain unverified.** Whether `Engineering Sciences` is shared by 8 or 11 engineering programs is a PRC syllabus question for the curator, and it only matters for Release B's family-expansion defaults.

### Verification owed — restructured 2026-08-04 into a post-deploy checkpoint

> **The gate as originally written is unachievable, and that is a fact about the deploy topology rather than a missed step.** R4 requires setting a Domain Context on a real note through the UI and regenerating. That needs `notes.domain_context` and the PR-3 authoring fields to exist in production. Production runs `main`, `main` is at **V101**, and every commit of this release sits on `releases/v0.69.0` — so R4 cannot run until the release is merged and deployed, which *is* the signoff step. The original instruction ("do this before PRs 4–7") was already overtaken: 4a, 4b and 7 shipped and R4 still could not run.
>
> **Resolution:** R4 becomes a dated post-deploy checkpoint, tracked in `docs/product/ROADMAP.md`'s Backlog Index as **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-08-18]`** with a named kill criterion. The steps below are unchanged; only when they run has moved.
>
> **Accepted risk, recorded rather than hidden.** If step 2 shows content drifting generic under a broader Domain Context, the fix amends ADR-001's ratified 8-value set — and that is now *more* expensive than it would have been before this release shipped: 43 notes carry `GENERAL_EDUCATION` via V104/V105, and PRs 1–4b and 7 all depend on the set. Shipping ahead of this verification is a deliberate trade, taken because the alternative is a release that can never close. **Bulk authoring must not begin until R4 passes** — that constraint is unchanged and is the kill criterion's first clause.

**PRs 1–4b and 7 are merged and every automated check passes, but the one risk that tests cannot cover is still unverified.** `01` risk R4: a Domain Context is often *broader* than the `course_program` it replaces (`Engineering Mathematics` vs. `Civil Engineering`), and a vaguer domain constraint can make generated content drift generic. No unit test detects this — the prompt-building tests assert which values reach the model, not whether the output is good.

This is an **owner/curator action through the real UI**, now possible since PR 3 shipped the authoring fields:

1. **Control.** Take an existing Civil Engineering note that already has a generated Study Pack. Set `domain_context = Civil Engineering`, `learner_level = Board Exam Review`. Regenerate and diff against the previous pack. Expect no meaningful quality change — the domain label is nearly identical to the program it replaced. If this one *does* change materially, the regression is in the wiring, not the taxonomy.
2. **The actual test of the architecture.** Take a `Strength of Materials` or `Algebra` note and set a deliberately broader context — `Engineering Sciences` or `Engineering Mathematics`. Regenerate. The content must stay engineering-specific and board-appropriate, **not** drift to generic textbook material.
3. **Level precedence.** Generate one quiz where the note's authored level is above the reader's profile level, and confirm the questions sit at the note's level with softened wording rather than dropping to the reader's.
4. **Senior High strand distinction — resolved 2026-08-03.** Production review found Physics notes under both ABM and STEM whose strand-specific framing would collapse under `GENERAL_EDUCATION`; the curator confirmed the recommendation to keep the strand in the authoring-domain fallback. V105 therefore sets only `learner_level = SENIOR_HIGH` and leaves `domain_context` null. The broader output-quality checks in steps 1–3 remain outstanding.

**Why this gates bulk authoring.** If step 2 shows drift, the fix is a **narrower Domain Context value set** — amending ADR-001's ratified 8-value list. Do this on one note before authoring eighty. Run it immediately after deploy, alongside the three production queries (`10`, `11`, `12`) that PR 5 and PR 6 need.

Recorded here because the step-by-step lived only in the (untracked) Codex prompts and in PR bodies #972/#973, so nothing in the repo said it was still owed — see `09-release-a-pr-sequence.md`'s PR 2 section, which describes the check as part of a now-merged PR's spec.

### Shipped

- **Pre-signoff pressure-test fix pass — five defects found by a whole-release review and fixed before signoff.** Two were blockers, and neither was visible to per-PR review. **(1) "Edit details" on the note detail page silently wiped Domain Context and Note Learner Level.** `PUT /notes/{id}` is a full replace, both update payloads on that page omitted the two fields, and `NoteService.update` writes every field unconditionally — so two clicks from the editor's own post-save redirect erased them, for any role, since copies inherit both. The payloads now carry them, and the TypeScript `UpsertNoteRequest` makes both fields **non-optional** so omission is a compile error rather than silent data loss — the same reasoning that deleted the backend silent-null convenience constructor in PR 3. The compiler found two further call sites. **(2) The reader's learner level leaked into static content.** `buildSubjectSuggestionGuidanceBlock` is concatenated into the static prompt but called `effectiveCurriculumLevel`, which falls back to the reader and then `COLLEGE`, so two users generating from byte-identical notes received different subject guidance. Pre-release, both guidance lists were emitted unconditionally and no reader-dependence existed — this release introduced it. It now reads the note's authored level directly, emits both lists when there is none, and the K-12 strand guard is back on the authoring domain rather than a level. **(3)** Static calibration referenced a "Curriculum level above" that was never emitted when neither axis resolved — the common path pre-backfill. **(4)** `targetLearnerLevel` lost its null sentinel: writing the resolved level floored it to `COLLEGE` and made the teacher Generate Quiz modal pre-fill a level nobody chose, locking later generations to it. Only an explicit Target Level is persisted again. **(5)** The edit-mode course/program fix from earlier in this release was incomplete at the display layer, so the footer advertised "Tailored for: X" while save rejected it. Also corrected the V104/V105 guard comments, which overstated what those guards detect — they catch a divergence between the UPDATE and guard predicates, not a shared wrong assumption about the production strings; that risk was retired by the audit queries, not by the guards.
- **Refreshed the Open Graph share image.** New card built around the current positioning ("Your notes become your study system") with the Note → Study Pack diagram, replacing the older "Build your notes library" artwork. Three things worth recording rather than rediscovering: the supplied PNG was **1536×1024 (3:2)** and would have been centre-cropped by Facebook/LinkedIn/X — cutting the brand lockup off the top and `notelib.app` off the bottom — so it was re-rendered from the SVG at the **1200×630** Open Graph ratio (95 KB, down from 607 KB). The source SVG carried a latent whitespace bug: trailing spaces inside `<tspan>` are collapsed by strict renderers though browsers keep them, silently producing "yourstudy system." and "notes intoStudy Packs"; `xml:space="preserve"` now pins it, with the reason in the file header. And the image is **versioned as `og-image-v2.png` rather than overwriting `og-image.png`**, because those platforms cache Open Graph images by URL and can serve a stale card for weeks — X has no public re-scrape tool — so a same-name swap would leave every already-shared link showing the old image. The previous `og-image.png` stays in place so already-scraped links that re-fetch do not 404. Alt text updated to match the new card.
- **Topic-note generation no longer rejects valid formula content.** `Create from topic` failed intermittently with `LLM_INVALID_OUTPUT` on formula-heavy material because two output contracts disagreed: the JSON schema allowed 240 characters per generated-note item while backend validation rejected any item over 28 whitespace-delimited words, and the prompt never mentioned the word ceiling. The reproduced rejection was a correct discharge formula with its variables defined — 199 characters, but 38 "words". `quickRecall` is now bounded by characters against the same constant the schema uses; `coreDetails` and `whyItMatters` keep the 28-word prose ceiling, since a word count is only wrong for notation. The prompt now states the bound, so the model is no longer judged against a rule it was never told — that mismatch is what produced the original four-of-five pass rate. This sat on the critical path: this release exists to unblock authoring Engineering Mathematics notes across eleven engineering programs, which are formula-heavy by definition. Full reproduction in `docs/claude-prompt/topic-note-quick-recall-validation-review.md`; the brief's secondary finding (topic generation never sends `subject`) is tracked separately and remains open.
- **Ambiguous High School and Senior High strand backfill (PR 4b of 7).** Applied the curator's content-based classification to 4 of the 10 legacy `High School` notes: three now carry `JUNIOR_HIGH` + `GENERAL_EDUCATION`, and one carries `SENIOR_HIGH` + `GENERAL_EDUCATION`. The review listed 11 notes; one classified note (`5afb255d…`, "Newton's Laws of Motion") was an exact byte-identical duplicate of `2a19d8ec…` and was deleted by the curator on 2026-08-03 before the migration was written, which is why this table has 10 rows against the query's documented 11. The other six are deliberately unclassifiable and retain both new axes as `NULL`; this preserves `High School` as their only legacy classification instead of inventing a grade level. The 11 Senior High strand notes now carry `SENIOR_HIGH` only: Domain Context stays `NULL`, so STEM, ABM, and HUMSS continue to reach generation through the retained `course_program` fallback. V105 never writes `course_program`.

  | Curator result | Note ID | Production note | Rationale |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `JUNIOR_HIGH` | `858ccbbe-61d2-4c23-b8db-9e702af08e88` | Photosynthesis Process and Importance | Classified from the note's actual content rather than from the ambiguous legacy label. |
    | `JUNIOR_HIGH` | `2a19d8ec-93db-4c08-b1cb-b2db77c2d980` | Newton's Laws of Motion | Classified from the note's actual content rather than from the ambiguous legacy label. |
  | `JUNIOR_HIGH` | `31066362-1616-4b1b-84b3-143ad5a4cc0c` | Invention and Function of the Light Bulb | Classified from the note's actual content rather than from the ambiguous legacy label. |
  | `SENIOR_HIGH` | `8726bf38-4343-45ed-a285-dc35db7b4ff9` | Pyroclastic Flow Dynamics and Hazards | Classified from the note's actual content rather than from the ambiguous legacy label. |
  | `UNCLASSIFIABLE` | `7411902d-1264-4fd0-9908-d422cdd9e862` | General biology | Written “for Software Engineers,” so it has tertiary/professional framing rather than a K-12 grade anchor. |
  | `UNCLASSIFIABLE` | `bdfaad4c-4341-4d61-abd8-3963a864f32f` | Volcanic Activity | Framed for “Geological and Engineering Contexts,” not a K-12 curriculum level. |
  | `UNCLASSIFIABLE` | `84d49a06-c46c-4fa3-aab8-a48aebe58f0f` | Mosquito Biting Preferences in Humans | General-interest science explainer with no grade anchor. |
  | `UNCLASSIFIABLE` | `9844846d-c0d1-4ef3-8528-e441bacd9c10` | Why Do Flying Termites and Insects Gather Around Lights at Night | General-interest science explainer with no grade anchor. |
  | `UNCLASSIFIABLE` | `e33f27fb-827d-4b38-a9db-c31858abef3a` | Why do roads smell different after rain? | General-interest science explainer with no grade anchor. |
  | `UNCLASSIFIABLE` | `2ec3253f-d51b-4541-87e7-873020bed467` | The Effectiveness of Teaching Others on Learning Retention | General-interest learning explainer with no grade anchor. |

- **Note editor no longer writes the editor's profile course/program onto an existing note.** `profileCourseProgram` was populated unconditionally while the `isEditMode` guard covered only the draft pre-fill, so editing a note whose `course_program` was null rendered an empty Course / Program field and silently submitted the editing author's own profile value on save — with no toast and no validation stop. The profile now pre-fills on **create only**; editing uses the note's own persisted value, and a note without one gets the normal "Please complete: Course / Program" prompt. An intentional behavior change: the preferred failure mode is asking the author to classify the note, not silently classifying it for them. Found while scoping PR 4, and the reason ADR-001's second corollary defers clearing `course_program` — the backfill would otherwise have created 38 more exposed notes.
- **Subject-equals-Domain-Context authoring nudge (PR 7 of 7).** When a Teacher or Admin author gives a note a `subject` identical to its selected Domain Context label, the note editor now shows an inline advisory under the Subject field suggesting the subject is probably too broad for a library shelf. Case- and whitespace-insensitive, exact match only — `Nursing Pharmacology` under `Nursing` is not flagged. Advisory only: never a validation error, never a save block, no new API call, and it renders only where the Domain Context select itself does. Targets the ~334 notes already carrying a program name as their subject.
- **Pure-level legacy-note backfill (PR 4a of 7).** Backfilled the 27 production notes labelled `Grade School` or `Junior High` with `GENERAL_EDUCATION` plus their exact note learner level, so future generation receives an academic domain and a separate curriculum level. Their existing `course_program` values are deliberately retained because clearing them is cosmetic once Domain Context wins the resolver and would make the migration irreversible. The 11 ambiguous `High School` notes and 11 Senior High strand notes were deliberately deferred to PR 4b, recorded above.
- **Canonical authoring surfaces — note editor, topic generation, and Bulk Generate (PR 3 of 7).** Teacher and Admin authors can now optionally set Domain Context and Note Learner Level in the shared note editor and for an entire Bulk Generate batch; topic-first note generation also receives the selected Domain Context. Both axes flow through the shared resolver, every created note, and the read-once bulk result receipt. Teacher quiz Target Level now replaces the curriculum slot without reader-scaffolding side effects, and the silent-null `UpsertNoteRequest` convenience constructor is removed.
- **Canonical generation context — resolver and prompt wiring (PR 2 of 7).** Domain Context now supplies the authoritative academic-domain constraint, while note learner level supplies authored depth for static content and the curriculum floor for quizzes/exams. The shared resolver owns both fallback chains; lower reader levels may soften scaffolding but never lower curriculum, higher reader levels never raise note difficulty, and quantitative/subject guidance now reads the effective domain and curriculum axes. The Review Set Companion path remains intentionally unchanged.
- **Canonical note authoring axes — persistence and transport foundation (PR 1 of 7).** Added nullable `notes.domain_context` and note-level `notes.learner_level`, the ratified closed `DomainContext` enum, create/update validation and explicit clearing, copy inheritance, and the complete detail/list/collection/profile/dashboard/data-export DTO surface. Private and public Library tuple projections now select and map both aliases, with real-SQL integration coverage; generation, prompts, filters, facets, UI, backfill, and pool/bank behavior remain unchanged for the focused follow-up PRs.

## v0.68.0 - Topic Note Rename

**Status: Released**

Theme: rename the bare-prompt "Generate Note" action to "Create a Note" so the word "Generate" stays reserved for the product's actual differentiator, plus six more ready, ungated items folded in before signoff: a related micro-copy grammar fix, the Companion Guidance Doctrine, a first slice of the newly-ratified Messaging Architecture, then — folded in 2026-08-01 on explicit owner review after the first three shipped — the two consistency batches the rename's own deliberately-narrow scope left behind (`plans.ts`'s pricing-table and upgrade-highlight strings, and the topic-note quota's user-facing vocabulary), and finally a docs-only correction to `fable-out/06`'s rename map, whose stale "keep" verdicts on two of the strings this release renamed would otherwise have set up a future session to revert them.

### Planned Scope

- **"Generate Note" → "Create a Note" rename (frontend only).** Ratified 2026-07-31 as Company Redefinition Phase 4, §4 item 6 (`docs/claude-prompt/company-redefinition-out/05-packaging-and-terminology.md`). Covers `note-editor-form.tsx`'s `generateNoteLabel` default, onboarding Step 3, the demo page, and downstream status/confirmation copy that names the same action ("Generating a note will replace the current content..." → "Creating a note will replace...", "Generating note…" → "Creating note…"). "Generate Study Pack," "Generate Quiz," and "Regenerate" (teacher note-detail flow) are explicitly unchanged — those transform the learner's own material, which is the mechanism this rename is protecting the word "Generate" for.
- **"Retry Generate" grammar fix (frontend only).** Company Redefinition Phase 4, §4 item 8 — a copy-quality oddity flagged but not decided at ratification. The Study Pack/Quiz generation-failure retry label reads "Retry Generate" in 7 locations across 3 files; fixed to "Retry Generate**ion**" (matching the file's own existing "you can retry generation" phrasing elsewhere). Stays in the *keep* bucket — no verb swap, since this is Study Pack/Quiz generation, not the topic-note action above.
- **Companion Guidance Doctrine (docs only).** Ratified/narrowed 2026-07-29, folded in 2026-07-31 since the doctrine text itself needs no further owner decision (docs/copy only, per its own Gate). Adds a new "Companion Guidance Doctrine" section to `AGENTS.md`: one learning responsibility per feature, one question per surface, and a de-duplication rule for future guidance-surface work. No rename, no new user-facing brand, no backend/analytics change.
- **Pricing page + Plus/Pro tagline re-messaging (frontend only).** First implementation slice of the Messaging Architecture initiative ratified 2026-08-01 (see `ROADMAP.md`'s Backlog Index — originated from Company Redefinition Phase 4 §4 item 4, widened after the initial Board-Exam-Mode-led drafts were reconsidered as narrowing NoteLib's identity). `plans.ts`'s `PLUS`/`PRO` `title`/`description` fields, and the pricing page's hero (`h1` + two supporting paragraphs + `<meta description>`) and second-section heading, move from exam-prep/feature-list framing to the ratified "Always know what to learn next" / "we sell the learning system" positioning. `name: "Plus"` / `name: "Pro"` unchanged everywhere — tagline only, per the ratified decision to avoid a bifurcated vocabulary at checkout/Settings/receipts. Scoped explicitly to this one surface; every other *positioning* surface (paywall modal, Exam Hub upsell, landing page, plan comparison table, feature bullets) stays untouched, each needing its own future scoping pass. **Scope amendment, recorded at signoff:** `app/settings/page.tsx` was in fact also changed by this batch (the plan-card alignment fix in `16e3bd1c`, disclosed in Shipped below but never reflected here), and at signoff `plans.ts`'s three plan `description` fields were rebalanced for length — so "one surface" was never accurate for the batch as shipped.

- **`plans.ts` rename-consistency leftovers (frontend only).** Folded in 2026-08-01. The rename above deliberately declared `plans.ts`'s pricing table out of scope, which left the `PLAN_COMPARISON_ROWS` row label `Topic note generation` and `FREE.upgradeHighlights`'s `Higher note generation limits` naming the renamed action with the retired verb, on the product's most-read commercial surface. Both move to the `topic note` vocabulary below. `FREE.title`'s stage-vs-outcome inconsistency was originally folded in alongside them and then dropped — see Shipped. Row *values* (`Limited`/`Higher`/`Highest`), all quota numbers, and every `pricingConfig` reference are unchanged.
- **Topic-note quota vocabulary → "topic note(s)" (frontend only).** Folded in 2026-08-01. 18 user-facing strings across `onboarding/page.tsx`, `note-editor-page-client.tsx`, `bulk-generation-page-client.tsx`, `library/page.tsx`, `lib/paywall-content.ts`, and `near-limit-banner.tsx` still called the renamed action's metered unit a "note generation" (the last two were missed by the scoping sweep — see the Shipped entry). Adopting **`topic note`** — the noun form of the `Create from topic` entry-tab label this release already shipped. Two alternatives were considered and rejected on evidence: `note creations` (awkward as a countable noun), and `note drafts` (actively wrong — `Draft` is already a user-visible state derived from `studyPackStatus === "DRAFT"`, spelled out to users at `library/page.tsx:1815` as "you've generated Study Packs for everything in your library," so a hand-written note is also a Draft and consumes no topic-note quota). `onboarding/page.tsx:1281`'s pre-existing "room to generate note drafts this month" is corrected by this batch rather than treated as precedent. Explicitly unchanged: `library/page.tsx:1461`'s bulk-failure outcome copy ("N of M notes generated. These couldn't be generated") — that describes what the backend did, not a quota unit; the bulk feature's own name (`Bulk generate notes`, and the Library create-menu item pointing at it), which the rename PR deliberately left alone; and every backend field name (`noteGenRemaining`, `noteGenerationsRemaining`).

Anti-drift: label/copy changes only, no behavior change to what any action does, no pricing/quota/checkout mechanics change — the quota *numbers* and gating logic are untouched, only the noun naming the metered unit. Does not touch Study Pack/Quiz generation terminology (only its grammar), or any other Company Redefinition §4 item left open at ratification (items 5 and 9 — see `ROADMAP.md`'s Company Redefinition Backlog Index row). **Item 7 (rollout sequencing) is touched at the documentation level only:** the `fable-out/06` staleness correction below fixes two stale "keep" verdicts in the rename map item 7 sequences, because leaving them would have set up a future revert of this release. No rename from that map was executed, and item 7's actual remaining substance — the unexecuted "AI" de-emphasis pass — stays unscoped and still needs its own `/kickoff`. **Scope amendment, recorded rather than applied silently:** this paragraph originally stated the release did not touch quota/paywall copy at all, and the rename bullet above still records `plans.ts`'s pricing table as unchanged — both were accurate for the work shipped up to 2026-08-01. The two batches added that day deliberately cross that boundary on explicit owner review; the rename's own Shipped bullet is left as an accurate record of what *that* PR did and was not rewritten. Companion Guidance Doctrine's own Phase 1+ (audit/merge of the 8 existing "what's next" resolvers) and the Messaging Architecture's remaining surfaces (paywall modal, Exam Hub upsell, landing page, plan-comparison "Best for" framing, feature bullets) both stay gated/unscoped — not part of this release. The landing hero (`app/page.tsx:185`, "Write, paste, or generate notes") was explicitly considered for this fold and left alone: it mixes a cheap verb fix with the expensive positioning question, and half-doing it is worse than leaving it.

### Shipped

- **"Generate Note" → "Create a Note" rename (frontend only).** Renamed the bare-topic action's CTA, loading/retry/helper copy, onboarding Step 3, demo flow, and fallback error copy (`note-editor-form.tsx`, `note-editor-page-client.tsx`, `app/onboarding/page.tsx`, `app/demo/page.tsx`, `lib/api.ts`) so "Generate" remains specific to transforming a learner's own note into a Study Pack/Quiz. Implemented by Codex from `docs/codex-prompts/v0.68.0-topic-note-rename.md`, then audited: the audit found and fixed one real gap the prompt's own file:line list had missed (onboarding Step 3's CTA button read "Create a Note" but its `loadingText` prop still said "Generating..."), and an `advisor()` second opinion (required for this Long-mode prompt) caught two more scope-adjacent gaps the original grep had missed entirely — the `Generate from topic` / `Create from topic` entry-tab label (`note-editor-form.tsx`, plus its 10 test assertions), and stale references in `docs/product/SPEC.md`. A follow-up sweep for every remaining "generate note" pattern in the repo found and fixed three more instances of the same duplicated-fallback-copy pattern (`app/onboarding/page.tsx`'s and `note-editor-page-client.tsx`'s own catch-block error strings, and the Library page's create-menu item description) plus five more stale doc references (`docs/features/study-pack-generation.md`, `bulk-generation.md`, `profile-learning-context.md`, `onboarding.md`, `docs/testing/onboarding.md`). Confirmed unchanged throughout: Study Pack/Quiz generation copy, all quota/limit/paywall copy, `plans.ts`'s pricing table, the bulk-generate feature, Study Pack `GENERATING`-status copy (a distinct, unrelated concept also containing the word "generating"), backend code, and historical RELEASES.md/archive entries (left as an accurate record of what shipped at the time). Full suite green throughout (162/162 suites, 1638/1638 tests), `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **"Retry Generate" → "Retry Generation" grammar fix (frontend only, Claude Code direct).** Fixed in `private-note-detail-page-client.tsx` (button label ×3 at `:350`, `:2126`, `:2167`; prose ×2 at `:2266`, `:2346`), plus one status-card `message` prop each in `flashcards-page-client.tsx:132` and `memorization-page-client.tsx:241` — 7 locations across 3 files. **Corrected at signoff:** this bullet previously said "5 locations" and described the flashcards/memorization change as "the same button label … and their shared helper prose." Neither file contains a retry button or any shared prose; each changed exactly one `message` prop. Existing tests updated to match; `tsc --noEmit` clean, all affected suites pass (76/76 tests across the 3 touched suites).
- **Companion Guidance Doctrine (docs only, Claude Code direct).** New `### Companion Guidance Doctrine` section added to `AGENTS.md` (placed near the existing Page Responsibility Rule, which the doctrine directly extends). `ROADMAP.md`'s Backlog Index row updated to record the doctrine text as adopted, distinct from the still-gated Phase 1 audit/merge work.
- **Pricing page + Plus/Pro tagline re-messaging (frontend only, Claude Code direct).** `plans.ts`: `PLUS.title`/`description` and `PRO.title`/`description` reframed around the learning-system outcome instead of exam-prep/feature-quota language. `pricing/page.tsx`: hero `h1` ("Always know what to learn next."), supporting paragraph, nav line, `<meta description>`, and the second-section `h2` all updated to match; the second section's own two supporting paragraphs (quota/mechanics info, not positioning claims) were deliberately left unchanged. Existing tests updated to match the new copy. Verified via a real dev-server render (`curl` against a running `next dev`, confirmed all four new strings render) plus a layout-safety check of the plan-card CSS (`grid` with default `align-items: stretch`, `CardTitle` has no `truncate`/`line-clamp`/fixed height) — the longer Pro/Plus titles wrap safely without misaligning the three-column card grid. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite green (162/162 suites, 1638/1638 tests). **Owner screenshot review of the live Settings billing page** (a third plan-card renderer this change didn't originally touch — `app/settings/page.tsx`, distinct from the `/pricing` page's `PricingPlansSection`) surfaced two real, pre-existing bugs made newly visible by Plus/Pro's longer titles: Free's title rendered at `text-2xl font-bold` while Plus/Pro used `text-lg font-semibold` (now unified on the smaller size), and the "Current plan" pill was stacked in the vertical flow above the plan name, so whichever card was active started lower than the other two — fixed by moving the pill into a `flex justify-between` row alongside the plan name (PRO's "Most popular" eyebrow uses the same slot when not current), matching the owner's suggested fix exactly. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full Settings suite passing (31/31). **Reconciled at the release-to-`main` merge:** the owner had ratified this initiative in parallel on a separate branch (PR #963, merged to `main` 2026-08-01 00:10) with a far more detailed Backlog Index row — the locked Hero → Supporting → Profile-bullets → Features hierarchy, the Spotify-Premium framing, illustrative per-profile bullets with `PROFESSIONAL` flagged aspirational-not-shippable, and the tagline-only naming decision. That row superseded and deleted the "External marketing copy" row, so this release's own thinner row was replaced by it rather than merged alongside. Two corrections fall out of that. **First: the `/pricing` hero and supporting paragraph shipped the ratified copy word for word**, and the `PLUS`/`PRO` taglines match the ratified strings exactly — verified after the fact, not coordinated in advance. **Second, and a correction to this release's own repeated claim:** the "do not change positioning copy without its own conversion-test discipline" bar was **not** left unretracted.
